# Full Body Force Generation — complete 36-class plan


36 classes · 12 stages · Athleticism Accelerator

Combine all twelve Upper Body Force Generation classes and all twelve Lower Body Force Generation classes, with upper and lower rotational power in every class. This is an ordered class sequence; no weekly frequency is assumed.

**Design rule:** Use existing exercises and arrange them around the day’s main effort. The design comes from selection, grouping and order. Do not create new drills or force variations for uniqueness.

Each class references the existing Access & Prepare 1 unchanged, then provides:

- 6 explosive exercises: 2 upper force, 2 lower force, 1 upper rotation and 1 lower rotation.
- 2 light stabilization/resilience exercises: 1 upper and 1 lower.
- 6 challenging primary-strength exercises: 2 upper force, 2 lower force and 1 direct support for each rotational region.

Across each three-class stage, every exercise from the matching upper and lower force classes is included at its original dose. Rotational additions receive smaller doses and full separate recovery. The third light slot per region is a justified repeat. All 336 original force prescriptions are covered; the complete plan contains 504 exercise entries.

Read the [complete 36-class manual](FULL_BODY_36_CLASS_PLAN.md), [framework and progression map](curriculum.md), [full-sequence audit](full_sequence_audit.md) and [athlete feedback record](athlete_feedback.md). The [source coverage record](source_coverage.json), [workload totals](sequence_workload.json), [exercise identities](exercise_identity_review.json) and [strength progression ledger](strength_progressions.md) retain the detail behind the plan.

## Use and progression

Begin with established basic lifting and landing technique, qualified supervision and individually suitable loads. Each class includes its own jobs, cues, replacements, recovery, same-lift preparation and quality criteria. Light supports keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve; primary work keeps about 2–3. Complete the six explosive tasks separately, with recovery between them.

Classes 16–18 consolidate with a smaller prescribed dose. Classes 34–36 revisit selected reference work; compare only documented matching conditions. Drill variety follows the intended job, with related variants and useful repeats disclosed. A new class is not evidence of readiness or permission to increase load.

Review actual attendance, technique, loads, symptoms, recovery and other training before delivery. Do not stack the standalone source classes on top of this combined plan or double up missed classes. The saved curriculum is complete; athlete performance remains unreported.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 1 of 36

**Stage 1, lesson A: Still-start upward and forward force**

**Primary effort:** Start a decisive upward leg push before a still-base forward release.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Start a decisive upward leg push before a still-base forward release.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Settled force onset:** Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start and Medicine Ball Chest Pass — stationary, paused: Settle the body and load before accelerating; use the same clear start discipline for upward leg projection and forward hand release.
- **E3 + E4 — Force through a stable finish:** Calf Raise — rapid supported rise, no flight and Band Row — seated, fast pull: Keep the support base organized while force builds toward the ankle-rise or arm-draw finish; reset without rocking the torso.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Split-stance explosive hip pivot and Med Ball Rotational Scoop Toss — paused standing start; floor-target adaptation: Settled rotational projection beside an isolated grounded hip/foot initiation.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start** | **2 × 3** | Main knee/hip force from a still start, expressed vertically. Hold a comfortable quarter squat 2 s with hands at hips; jump vertically without another dip, land on both feet with yielding knees and hold 2 s. Keep jump height within a controlled reception. Use instead: a smaller same-start jump; if flight cannot be controlled, a fast nonflight squat rise at the same dose and zero landings. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E2 | **Medicine Ball Chest Pass — stationary, paused** | **3 × 3** | Main straight-line release practice. Face forward, feet parallel, soft knees, ball at chest. Hold still for 1 second, then punch both hands forward together; no dip, step or twist. Reset 20 seconds between attempts, including retrieval; rest 90 seconds between sets. Record fixed feet, quiet trunk and straight-line release within these attempts. Use instead: a lighter, grippable ball with the same stationary release and remaining dose; if no contained release is feasible, use rapid light-dumbbell floor presses for remaining attempts with the same resets. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E3 | **Calf Raise — rapid supported rise, no flight** | **1 × 4** | Express a quick ankle finish while keeping forefeet continuously grounded. With stable hand support and soft-straight knees, rise onto forefeet quickly, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to floor. No jump or bouncing heel drop. Use instead: a smaller unloaded quick heel rise with the same grounded dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E4 | **Band Row — seated, fast pull** | **1 × 4** | Keep accelerating the horizontal draw as elastic resistance increases toward the finish. Sit tall, knees slightly bent; use an intact band securely around the mid-soles of both shod feet, only in a manufacturer-permitted setup. Pull quickly to lower ribs; return for 2 seconds and reset for 2 seconds without rocking. Hands stay below chest height; inspect for slip before loading. Use instead: a lighter intact band at the same remaining dose; if the permitted foot anchor cannot be secured, use a light bilateral dumbbell hinge row with the same rapid draw and controlled return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 2 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E5 | **Split-stance explosive hip pivot** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Isolate the support-foot release and rapid hip-turn initiation before adding a swinging leg. Start in a short split stance. Rapidly shift toward the lead leg and turn feet and pelvis together through a comfortable arc of about 45 degrees or less. Both forefeet remain grounded as the heels may release. Finish still for 2 seconds. Mirror stance and direction for the other side; no kick or jump. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: Reduce the turn size if a foot sticks, knee twists or the trunk leads. Stop the task if the surface does not permit a controlled foot-and-hip pivot. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Med Ball Rotational Scoop Toss — paused standing start; floor-target adaptation** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Generate rotational projection from a settled load. Stand side-on at comfortable athletic width with a light ball beside the rear hip. Pause 2 seconds, pivot the rear foot, turn hips and trunk, then sweep both hands into the low forward target. No bounce, countermovement or step. Hold the balanced finish for 2 seconds. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: Reduce ball mass or turn range for arm-first movement or loss of balance; retain the paused grounded scoop. If the release area cannot be qualified, stop and re-audit this slot rather than throw into an unsafe area. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Scapular Push-Up — quadruped, dynamic** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Strengthen controlled shoulder-blade travel against light bodyweight so the chest-pass finish and push-up support do not depend on elbow bending or trunk sag. Hands and knees remain down; keep elbows straight, let the chest settle slightly, then push the floor away. Use 2 seconds inward, 2 seconds outward and a 2-second finish hold. Stop for shrugging, elbow bend or lumbar movement. Use instead: a smaller controlled shoulder-blade excursion at the same easy dose; do not turn it into an elbow-bending push-up. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| S2 | **Tibialis Raise — wall-supported** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light ankle dorsiflexor loading to control foot position before a stable push/landing. Back against an inspected wall, heels on floor; lift forefeet 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Adjust foot distance to stay easy. Use instead: a seated unweighted toe raise through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Goblet Squat — dumbbell** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Bilateral knee/hip extension through a repeatable full-foot push. Lower 3 s, stand decisively in about 1 s; hold a manageable goblet load and retain comfortable depth without heels lifting. Use instead: a lighter goblet squat through controlled comfortable range; a two-dumbbell front hold may replace a limiting goblet grip. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P2 | **Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main loaded horizontal push: develop force through a reproducible floor-limited pressing range with a reproducible lower boundary. Lie supine, knees bent, feet planted; coach supervises pickup and set-down. Lower for 2 seconds until upper arms touch gently, pause 1 second without relaxing, then press decisively. No bridge, wrist collapse or bouncing. Rest 2½ minutes between working sets. Use instead: lighter dumbbells and the same comfortable floor-limited range while preserving the stated reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 150 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P3 | **Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force with the knees extended for the final upward/forward push. Use stable hand support if needed; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to the floor. Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the backward draw at a wider upper-arm angle, supporting control of the arm-open position preceding a chest pass. This complements the source exercise “One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench”'s tucked-elbow pull. Hold a steady hip hinge, elbows about 60 degrees from the torso; draw toward upper ribs, pause 1 second, lower for 2 seconds. End before the shoulder rolls forward or the back starts lifting the weight. Use instead: lighter dumbbells and a smaller controlled rear-delt row range; keep the hinge still. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 1 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 1 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Goblet Squat — dumbbell:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5 then 1 × 3; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 6 then 1 × 3. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Seated band hip internal rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 9 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 6 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (12 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 4 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 8 rounds / 9 side-specific bouts, 56 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 36 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 12 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 67–73 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start establishes the still-start vertical-force reference, paired with Medicine Ball Chest Pass — stationary, paused for a still-base forward release. The grounded hip pivot and paused rotational scoop add turn initiation without a kick or additional foot reception. Prescribed baseline: 13 ball releases, 6 bilateral foot-landing events, 0 bilateral hand-receiving events. Before delivery, review actual recent loading and recovery; require the held squat start without a second dip, a controlled two-foot finish and a quiet-trunk chest release. If flight cannot be controlled, use the listed fast nonflight squat rise for only the remaining E1 attempts and record removed landings; keep full resets and add no test attempts.

This is the first combined prescription; the source courses are written references, not evidence that these athletes completed them. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_01_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_01.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 2; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 2 of 36

**Stage 1, lesson B: Grounded hip drive and fast traction**

**Primary effort:** Drive the hips sharply while expressing a quick arm pull and controlled hand push-off.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Drive the hips sharply while expressing a quick arm pull and controlled hand push-off.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Force from a parked load:** Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause and Kettlebell Swing — dead-stop two-hand repetitions: Organize the held starting load before accelerating; preserve the row's still trunk and the swing's deliberate hip drive as separate ways of producing force.
- **E3 + E4 — Impulse and controlled reception:** Plyo Push-Up — hands elevated and Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — hip-driven forward release: Direct a decisive push through the support before the body or ball moves away, then control the body's finish; only the push-up includes a hand reception.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Standing low roundhouse shadow kick and Half-Kneeling Rotational Med Ball Throw — floor-target adaptation: Reduce upper leg propulsion in the throw while directly expressing the lower hip-led kick.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause** | **2 × 3** | Produce rapid bilateral pulling force from a stationary arm position against implement inertia. Straddle the bar, hinge and grip the sleeve securely with both hands; keep the trunk still. Pull quickly toward lower ribs without hip drive; lower for 2 seconds, then hold the extended-arm start for 3 seconds before the next pull. Keep the load in hand. Rest 90 seconds between sets. Use a light bar/load that actually moves quickly. Use instead: a light seated bilateral band row with a 3 s settled extended-arm start at the same remaining dose/rest if the minimum landmine load or secure grip is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 3 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E2 | **Kettlebell Swing — dead-stop two-hand repetitions** | **2 × 3** | Rapid hip extension moves a retained implement without adding landings. Hike a light kettlebell from a stable parked start, snap hips to a tall finish with a chest-level float, guide the return and park fully after each rep. No squat pull, arm lift or lumbar arch. Use instead: a very light rapid dumbbell hip hinge with a 2 s lowering and still finish if swing skill or grip is unconfirmed. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E3 | **Plyo Push-Up — hands elevated** | **1 × 3** | Express a brief, fast closed-chain push and accept the return through both arms. Use an inspected, immovable bench or box at a height allowing a small simultaneous hand release. Lower under control, reverse promptly, push off, then receive softly with elbows yielding. Reset 10 seconds between reps; no clap or repeated bouncing. Use instead: a higher inspected hand support for the same small release; if no controlled hand flight is possible, use a rapid incline push-up without flight for remaining reps and record removed hand contacts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E4 | **Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — hip-driven forward release** | **1 × 3** | Transfer leg/hip extension into a freely released implement rather than a retained swing. Light ball between thighs, soft knees and hinge; drive hips tall and project forward/upward with long arms. Feet stay down, no trunk turn or catching return. Let the ball stop before retrieval. Use instead: three rapid light-dumbbell quarter-squat rises with heels grounded, 10 s between reps and the same next-section recovery; keep arms fixed and drive the load with the legs; log zero throws. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E5 | **Standing low roundhouse shadow kick** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Express lower-body rotational force through a fast hip-led low kick, then actively recover the leg and stance. Start in a short fighting stance with the kicking leg rear. Turn the support foot with the pelvis, lift and sweep the bent thigh, then extend only through a controllable low arc. Actively recoil and return to the starting stance, holding the finish for 2 seconds. Keep the foot below hip height and the terminal knee soft. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a shorter low thigh arc with less terminal extension if balance or active recoil fails. If only slow rehearsal can be qualified, record technique-only delivery rather than explosive completion. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Half-Kneeling Rotational Med Ball Throw — floor-target adaptation** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Express trunk-to-hand acceleration with leg propulsion reduced. Set the target-side foot forward with the opposite knee comfortably supported on the floor. Begin the light ball beside the rear hip. Use a small comfortable thoracic turn and fast two-hand release toward the low target. Keep the front foot planted and pelvis quiet without forcing a rigid pelvis through a larger trunk twist. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: Shorten the turn or use a lighter ball if the knee shifts or back arches. If kneeling contact is intolerable, use a seated bench rotational throw with a small comfortable trunk turn instead. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Bilateral Band External Rotation — elbows at sides** | **1 × 8** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Apply light shoulder-rotator loading while controlling the upper-arm position used in pressing and rowing. Hold an unanchored light band between hands; elbows near 90 degrees at the ribs. Rotate forearms outward for 2 seconds, hold 1 second, return for 2 seconds. Stop for wrist substitution, elbow drift or shoulder discomfort. Use instead: a lighter band or smaller outward rotation at the same easy dose, with elbows at the ribs. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| S2 | **Side-Lying Hip Abduction — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light lateral-hip strengthening to keep the pelvis and knee organized over the force-producing leg. Lie on side, lower leg bent; lift upper leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s with toes facing forward and pelvis still. Bodyweight is sufficient unless clearly easy. Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P2 | **One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench** | **2 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main loaded horizontal pull: give each arm challenging shoulder-extension and elbow-flexion work while support limits trunk rotation. Back stays level; draw elbow toward hip without pulling it far behind the trunk. Lift decisively, hold 1 second, lower for 2 seconds. Complete left, rest 60 seconds, then right; rest 2 minutes before the second round. 24 repetitions total. Use instead: a lighter dumbbell and stable hand support through the same controlled row range; no unsupported torso twist. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 60 s between arm bouts; 24 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 3 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Give elbow extension a small, direct strength dose for the final pressing action without another full pressing compound. Lie supine, one dumbbell in each hand, neutral grip, upper arms near vertical. Bend elbows to lower the dumbbells beside the ears for 2 seconds, then extend under control; do not move the shoulders to finish. Coach controls setup and clearance. Use instead: lighter dumbbells with the same floor-supported elbow-extension action and comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| P5 | **Standing Cable or Band Hip Flexion** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Challenge active chamber lifting and controlled lowering so the free leg can accelerate and recover while the other leg supplies the rotational base. Stand upright with a secure band loop at the forefeet, the support foot anchoring its end and optional light fingertip contact on a stable support. Raise the bent knee to a comfortable low chamber with the pelvis quiet, then lower without swinging. Use about 1 second lifting, 2 seconds lowering and 1 second resetting. Do not use momentum, a hop or an unsecured loop. Use instead: Reduce tension or chamber height for pelvic tipping or loss of foot security. If the loop cannot be secured, use self-resisted seated bent-knee hip flexion with the hands resisting the distal thigh instead; record the changed, less reproducible resistance. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 1 · P5](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Cable / Band Lift — standing split-stance band execution** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the low-to-high turning path from the hip to the hands and control its return, supporting the selected rising or scooping upper rotational action. Secure a band at a verified low anchor behind one side. Stand in a comfortable split stance. Guide both hands from the rear hip across toward the opposite upper chest, allowing a small foot-and-hip turn. Drive in 1–2 seconds and return in 3 seconds. Keep ribs stacked and stop below any range that requires leaning back or shrugging. No cable machine is assumed. Use instead: Reduce resistance or range if the athlete leans back or loses a controlled return. If a suitable secure low anchor is unavailable, stop and re-audit the slot; do not improvise an unsafe anchor. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 1 · P5](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 1 cycles; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Standing Cable or Band Hip Flexion:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Cable / Band Lift — standing split-stance band execution:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 3 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 0 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (0 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 3 bilateral hand-receptions / 6 hand contacts. 6 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 4 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 8 rounds / 12 side-specific bouts, 64 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 3 complete walkout cycles (24 heel placements). Preparation: 32 reps/traverses plus 1 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 18 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 68–75 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause moves the observation to rapid traction from a settled load; Kettlebell Swing — dead-stop two-hand repetitions supplies deliberate hip acceleration from a parked bell. This separates trunk-still arm force from hip-driven force, while Plyo Push-Up — hands elevated introduces a small hand-receiving dose and the lower rotation slot introduces a low actively recoiled kick. Compared with Class 1, prescribed ball releases change from 13 to 7, bilateral foot-landing events change from 6 to 0, bilateral hand-receiving events change from 0 to 3. With actual recovery reviewed, require a steady row hinge, a controlled bell park and soft hand reception before another effort. If the landmine load or grip is unsuitable, use the listed seated bilateral band row with its settled start for remaining row attempts; if hand reception is unqualified, use the listed nonflight incline push-up for remaining E3 work and log zero hand receptions.

Previous combined Class 1 prescribed 9 primary bouts and 56 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 12 bouts, 64 reps and 3 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_02_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_02.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 3; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 3 of 36

**Stage 1, lesson C: Lead-leg projection and independent release**

**Primary effort:** Use a controlled lateral plant to initiate grounded hip rotation and a diagonal upper-body finish. Retain forward leg and independent arm projection as supporting force work.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Lateral step-in to rotational plant as the grounded rotational force anchor. Convert arriving lateral bodyweight into a grounded foot-and-hip turn with a controlled split-stance finish.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Lateral step-in to rotational plant. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Lateral step-in to rotational plant and Med Ball Overhead to Side Slam — non-rebounding slam-ball execution: Downward arm finish plus lateral bodyweight accepted into a grounded hip turn.
- **E3 + E4 — Preload into forward projection:** Standing Broad Jump — dip, project and stick and Medicine Ball Chest Pass — shallow countermovement: Use the declared small preload to direct force forward, finishing balanced without an extra approach or torso turn.
- **E5 + E6 — Lead-side support and finish:** Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins and Medicine Ball Shot Put Throw — forward-facing, no rotation: Maintain pressure through the declared support stance and finish balanced as the lead leg rises or the independent arm releases.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Lateral step-in to rotational plant** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Convert arriving lateral bodyweight into a grounded foot-and-hip turn with a controlled split-stance finish. From a parallel athletic stance, take one brisk short lateral step onto the receiving foot, then turn the feet and pelvis together up to 45 degrees into a shallow split kick stance. Let the receiving heel release and the other foot realign. Hold the finish for 2 seconds. Keep the step and finish within 2 metres, with another 2 metres clear for recovery. No kick, run-up or flight. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: Shorten the step and turn angle if the athlete overstrides, shuffles again or traps the knee. Both feet must be able to realign with the pelvis. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E5](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Med Ball Overhead to Side Slam — non-rebounding slam-ball execution** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Accelerate the hands diagonally downward through a turn and arrest the body's follow-through. From a comfortable overhead position, turn through the feet and hips and drive a light, verified non-rebounding, impact-compatible slam ball to the floor slightly forward and outside the target-side foot, clear of the toes. Keep the trunk long; do not fold and twist the low back. Wait until the ball stops, then lift and reset deliberately. Use instead: a shoulder-height diagonal side slam instead if overhead range requires rib flare. A non-rebounding, impact-compatible ball and contained permitted impact area remain required. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E6](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Standing Broad Jump — dip, project and stick** | **1 × 3** | Direct a coordinated leg push forward, distinct from the vertical still-start task. One controlled dip and arm swing; jump forward from both feet and land on both feet, hold 2 s. Keep start and landing within the marked first 3 m and remaining 7 m clear. Use instead: a shorter two-foot projection, or a rapid nonflight squat rise if reception cannot stay organized; record removed landings. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E4 | **Medicine Ball Chest Pass — shallow countermovement** | **1 × 3** | Connect a small preload to the same forward release. Unlike the source exercise “Medicine Ball Chest Pass — stationary, paused”'s still start, use one shallow knee/hip dip with a small ball draw toward the chest, then an immediate forward drive. Keep feet planted and shoulders facing forward; no jump, run-up or deep squat. Reset 20 seconds between attempts. Use instead: a smaller dip and lighter ball; if no contained release is feasible, use grounded rapid standing light-band presses with the same single small dip, remaining dose and resets. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E5 | **Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Let one lead leg supply upward force against a stable support without hand-off to a jump. Use a low inspected immovable step. Front whole foot planted; rise quickly without springing from the trailing foot, bring that foot onto the step, then descend under control in 3 s. No flight. Use instead: a supported fast split-squat rise with both feet grounded and the same per-leg dose if support fit fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |
| E6 | **Medicine Ball Shot Put Throw — forward-facing, no rotation** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Give each arm independent forward-projecting work while the trunk resists turning. Use a light, controllable ball at the throwing-side shoulder; the other hand supports setup then releases before the throw. Fixed split stance, opposite foot forward; no step or deliberate rotational preload. Reset 20 seconds between throws and rest 60 seconds before changing arm and lead foot. 4 throws total. Use instead: a lighter ball or shorter release range; if release containment fails, use a rapid one-arm light-dumbbell floor press with the same remaining per-arm dose/rest. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 1 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_01.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose. Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.
