## Full Body Force Generation — Class 12 of 36

**Stage 4, lesson C: Knee-to-ankle drive through the press finish**

**Primary effort:** Initiate controlled hip internal rotation and produce a lead-hand rotational pushing release. Grounded knee-to-ankle drive and linear arm presses supply supporting initiation and finishing force.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Seated light-band hip-rotation acceleration as the grounded rotational force anchor. Give the hip internal rotators a small rapid force-expression task without the balance or whole-kick demand.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Seated light-band hip-rotation acceleration. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Seated light-band hip-rotation acceleration and Lead-hand rotational medicine-ball push throw: Lead-hand rotational projection plus repeat seated hip torque avoids inventing a third rotating lower task; Class 4 pure chamber/recoil drills are not themselves rotation.
- **E3 + E4 — Extension through a controlled boundary:** Squat-to-Calf Rise — rapid extension, feet stay grounded and Floor Press — light dumbbells, fast concentric from floor contact: Carry force through the intended knee/ankle or arm extension, retaining the floor boundary and avoiding a back arch to finish.
- **E5 + E6 — Independent contribution with a steady trunk:** Reverse Lunge — quick front-leg return, no flight and Band Chest Press — one arm presses while opposite arm holds: Keep the support pressure and trunk organized while the front leg returns or one arm presses, preserving control of the opposite support or arm.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Seated light-band hip-rotation acceleration** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Give the hip internal rotators a small rapid force-expression task without the balance or whole-kick demand. Sit with thighs supported, hips/knees near 90 degrees and a very light loop at the lower shins. Start still, then move one foot a short distance outward briskly by rotating at the hip; knee/pelvis stay quiet. Return slowly; no bouncing or forced end range. Use instead: self-resistance from the opposite hand instead if no suitable loop fits, or reduce range/tension for knee movement; speed is controlled, never a ballistic joint wrench. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 3 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_03.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Lead-hand rotational medicine-ball push throw** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Express force through the arm nearest the target without a large rear-arm wind-up. Use a very light ball held at the lead-side chest with the other hand assisting its support. Make a small turn toward the lane and extend the lead arm into the low target; the supporting hand releases. Keep both feet planted/pivoting. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: a smaller grippable ball or shorten the turn if the lead shoulder rolls forward. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 4 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_04.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Squat-to-Calf Rise — rapid extension, feet stay grounded** | **1 × 3** | Sequence knee/hip extension into an ankle finish without leaving the floor. From a comfortable quarter squat rise quickly to tall legs, then onto forefeet in one smooth action; pause 1 s and lower under control. Do not jump or snap knees backward. Use instead: a smaller fast squat rise with heels remaining down if the ankle finish disrupts alignment. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| E4 | **Floor Press — light dumbbells, fast concentric from floor contact** | **1 × 4** | Accelerate an external load from a reproducible still start while the trunk is supported. Lower 2 s, settle upper arms gently for 1 s without losing tension, press quickly; keep grip throughout and decelerate before elbow lock. Use instead: a light self-anchored Band Chest Press if dumbbell handling cannot remain controlled. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 4 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| E5 | **Reverse Lunge — quick front-leg return, no flight** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Drive upward through the front leg after a controlled rear step. Bodyweight; step back quietly and lower 2 s, then drive quickly through the front whole foot to stand. No jumping switch or rear-toe launch. Complete two returns per lead leg. Use instead: a fast supported stationary split-squat rise at the same per-leg dose if stepping causes loss of alignment. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| E6 | **Band Chest Press — one arm presses while opposite arm holds** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Create a forward pulse with one arm while the other maintains a steady press position. Self-anchored band across upper back, securely held; perform two pulses with one arm while the other stays comfortably extended during each pulse. Release both arms during resets; change working arm after the side rest. Keep ribs and shoulders facing forward. Use instead: a self-anchored single-arm band speed press with the nonworking hand securing the tail at the torso if the opposite-arm hold causes fatigue. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Bilateral Band External Rotation — elbows at sides** | **1 × 8** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light shoulder-rotator strengthening to control upper-arm position during the day's press and pull. Unanchored band between hands, elbows at ribs; rotate outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s without shrugging. Use instead: a lower-tension band and shorter comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| S2 | **Side-Lying Hip Adduction — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light medial-hip force to maintain a straight stance-leg push without drifting away from the foot. Top foot rests in front of the lower knee. Lift the straight lower leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; pelvis remains stacked. Use instead: a smaller controlled lower-leg lift at the same dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Reverse Lunge — two dumbbells** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Accept a controlled step and drive through the front leg to regain the start. Step back quietly, lower under control and push through the whole front foot to stand; about 4 s per complete cycle. Do not launch off the trailing toes. Use instead: a stationary split squat at the same per-leg dose if stepping reduces control. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| P2 | **Push-Up — controlled full repetition** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Second major press: load force from the bottom while shoulder blades move freely and the trunk remains fixed. Lower 2 s, push decisively, finish tall through shoulders for 1 s; no hand flight. Adjust leverage or band resistance to reach 2–3 RIR. Use instead: a hands-elevated push-up on an inspected immovable support. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| P3 | **Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force with the knees extended for the final upward/forward push. Use stable hand support if needed; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to the floor. Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 4 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_04.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 4 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_04.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotational Press** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a compound unilateral push for the full-body force work while loading the connection from a freely turning foot and hip to a controlled arm press. Its diagonal pressing path complements the selected rotational task; it does not reproduce every throw path. Secure the landmine and qualify its bar, grip and minimum load. In a modest staggered stance, hold the sleeve near the rear shoulder with the rear hand. Turn the rear foot and hips into a controlled forward-and-up press, keeping wrist stacked and ribs level. No dip-drive, jump or release. Return in 3 seconds and reset without shoulder shrug or lower-back arch. Use instead: Reduce load or range for wrist, elbow or rib-position faults. If the minimum landmine setup is unsuitable, use a one-arm rotational band press from a verified anchor instead; log the changed load and setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 4 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_04.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Seated light-band hip-rotation acceleration returns to direct supported hip-rotation acceleration as the rotational anchor, while Lead-hand rotational medicine-ball push throw changes the upper endpoint to a lead-hand pushing release. Grounded knee-to-ankle extension and supported linear presses supply initiation and finishing force without adding foot or hand reception. Compared with Class 11, prescribed ball releases change from 3 to 4, bilateral foot-landing events change from 3 to 0, airborne feed catches change from 3 to 0, explosive set/side bouts change from 8 to 10. After reviewing actual recovery, require hip motion without knee/ankle substitution and a lead-hand release without the shoulder rolling forward. Reduce the seated range or tension for remaining E1 attempts, using the listed self-resistance only if loop fit fails; use a smaller grippable ball or shorter turn for remaining E2 attempts. Retain the side rests and log changed loading.

Previous combined Class 11 prescribed 9 primary bouts and 46 conventional/traverse reps plus 3 cycles; this class prescribes 10 bouts, 58 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. This third lesson completes stage coverage and repeats the selected light supports for the stated shoulder/hip/foot jobs. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_12_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_12.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 13; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 13 of 36

**Stage 5, lesson A: Receive, redirect and stick**

**Primary effort:** Convert a small received load or contact into the next force action, then regain control.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Broad Jump — two linked projections, final stick as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Convert a small received load or contact into the next force action, then regain control.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Broad Jump — two linked projections, final stick. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Receive and redirect force:** Broad Jump — two linked projections, final stick and Medicine Ball Receive-and-Return Chest Pass — single coach feed: Use the first controlled reception to organize the next projection, then regain control; keep the ball feed and the broad-jump contact small enough to redirect cleanly.
- **E3 + E4 — Controlled return into the finish:** Bent-Knee Calf Raise — single-leg rapid supported rise and Band Lat Pulldown — controlled upward reach into fast draw: Use a controlled lowering or loaded reach before the rapid ankle or arm finish, with the support base stable and no trunk swing.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Single-shuffle low roundhouse and Med Ball Shuffle-to-Rotation Throw — short floor-target version: Both endpoints express rotation after one small lateral arrival, with separate stations and full resets.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Broad Jump — two linked projections, final stick** | **2 × 2** | Main forward leg drive repeated once after accepting the first landing. Each round is exactly two jumps: first from a controlled dip, receive with both feet, then make one modest forward jump without an extra bounce; hold the second landing 2 s. Keep distances small and both receptions controlled. Use instead: two separate broad jumps with a 15 s reset per round if the first reception is not organized; do not add attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E2 | **Medicine Ball Receive-and-Return Chest Pass — single coach feed** | **2 × 3** | Primary conversion of a small received load directly into forward release. Coach supplies a gentle chest-height feed. Yield briefly at elbows, then return the ball forward without an intentional pause; one feed/return per rep, followed by full reset. No surprise or hard feeds. Use instead: the Class 4 catch-set chest pass if receiving control is unreported or the first feed is not clean; if catching itself is unsuitable use a self-started chest pass and record zero catches. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E3 | **Bent-Knee Calf Raise — single-leg rapid supported rise** | **1 × 3 per leg** | Produce quick stance-ankle force while holding a modest knee bend. Use stable hand support, keep one foot on the floor and knee softly bent; rise quickly onto the forefoot, hold 1 s and lower 2 s. Forefoot stays grounded. Use instead: an assisted bilateral bent-knee rapid raise for the same set/rep count if needed; record changed laterality. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 6 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E4 | **Band Lat Pulldown — controlled upward reach into fast draw** | **1 × 4** | Link an overhead loaded reach to a quick elbow-and-shoulder pull. Use a rated overhead attachment and tall kneeling base; allow hands upward for 2 s, then reverse smoothly into a quick pull toward ribs. No torso crunch. Use instead: a paused light Band Lat Pulldown if reversal control is absent. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E5 | **Single-shuffle low roundhouse** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Directly convert arriving lateral mass into support-foot/pelvic rotation. From a kick stance, make one short lateral shuffle toward the support side, settle that foot without an extra stutter and launch the opposite low roundhouse. Recoil and recover the stance. Entry and finish stay within 2 m; no target or continuous shuffling. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a shorter/slower single shuffle instead if the plant overstrides or the knee cannot turn freely; maintain a brisk controlled hip action. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 5 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Med Ball Shuffle-to-Rotation Throw — short floor-target version** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Accept lateral arrival and send the turn through the hands. Start side-on; take one short uncrossed shuffle toward the target direction, plant the lead foot and make a two-hand rotational scoop release. Stay grounded and finish within the marked bay. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Keep all entry and finish footwork inside a cleared 1-metre start bay; no hop, jump or sprint. Use instead: a single side-step into the throw instead if uncrossed shuffle placement is unreliable; same dose. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 5 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |

Hands at comfortable chest height, elbows softly fixed; separate hands 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s without shrugging or rib flare. Use instead: a smaller-range pull-apart with a lighter band. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |

Light medial-hip force to maintain a straight stance-leg push without drifting away from the foot. Top foot rests in front of the lower knee. Lift the straight lower leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; pelvis remains stacked. Use instead: a smaller controlled lower-leg lift at the same dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| P1 | **Goblet Squat — dumbbell** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Bilateral knee/hip extension through a repeatable full-foot push. Lower 3 s, stand decisively in about 1 s; hold a manageable goblet load and retain comfortable depth without heels lifting. Use instead: a lighter goblet squat through controlled comfortable range; a two-dumbbell front hold may replace a limiting goblet grip. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P2 | **Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main horizontal pressing force with a reproducible floor-limited range. Lower 2 s, touch upper arms gently, pause 1 s, then press decisively; feet stay down. Coach supervises pickup/set-down. Use instead: a lighter floor press through controlled comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 150 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P3 | **Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force while the knee remains flexed during early push and receiving. Sit on an inspected stable bench, feet on floor; secure loads on thighs, rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without knee-angle change or bouncing. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee calf raise with the same bilateral dose and 2–3 technical RIR if bench/load support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Strengthen the wider-elbow backward draw that controls the arm-open position. Hold a still hinge; elbows about 60 degrees from torso, pull to upper ribs, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Use instead: a chest-supported version only on an inspected bench with adequate arm clearance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 5 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 5 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. ## Full Body Force Generation — Class 14 of 36

**Stage 5, lesson B: Preload through supported limbs**

**Primary effort:** Use a small preload to drive through a supported arm or leg without unnecessary travel.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Use a small preload to drive through a supported arm or leg without unnecessary travel.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Coordinate a permitted preload:** Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted and Countermovement Jump — hands fixed at hips: Use the declared preload to sequence force into the jump or guided press, finishing without an extra dip or turn.
- **E3 + E4 — Independent force with stable support:** Medicine Ball Shot Put Throw — controlled arm draw into forward release and Single-Leg Hip Hinge — rapid return with hand support: Keep the supporting limb and trunk organized while the arm reverses to release or the hip returns from its hinge; use only the specified arm draw or hip motion.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Crossover entry to rotational knee chamber and Drop-step rotational medicine-ball scoop throw: Rear upper loading step and lower crossover-to-chamber differentiate how weight arrives before rotation.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted** | **1 × 3** | Connect a small leg preload to a forward/upward arm drive without a jump. Hold sleeve at upper chest with both hands; dip slightly, extend legs and immediately finish the press. Lower under control; no re-bend catch or bar release. Use instead: a two-hand strict landmine speed press if dip-to-arm sequencing is not controlled, or a light band chest press if the bar is too heavy. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 8 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E2 | **Countermovement Jump — hands fixed at hips** | **1 × 3** | Express upward knee/hip force without an arm swing masking the leg strategy. Keep hands at hips, make one comfortable dip and jump vertically; land on both feet and hold 2 s. Do not compare height with arm-assisted jumps. Use instead: a smaller hands-at-hips vertical jump or nonflight rapid squat rise at the same remaining dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E3 | **Medicine Ball Shot Put Throw — controlled arm draw into forward release** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Use a small upper-arm preload to accelerate a unilateral forward throw without trunk rotation. Face forward with feet fixed; bring the light ball slightly forward, draw it smoothly back toward the shoulder, then immediately push forward to release. No side-on wind-up. Use instead: a stationary forward-facing single-arm throw if the arm draw causes rotation or loss of ball control; use a secured single-arm band press if ball fit or containment fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E4 | **Single-Leg Hip Hinge — rapid return with hand support** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Drive the hip from a lengthened position on one leg without adding a landing. Hold stable support, hinge a short controlled range for 2 s with free leg reaching behind, then return tall quickly without arching or pulling on the support. Bodyweight first. Use instead: a kickstand rapid hinge return at the same per-leg dose if pelvis control is lost. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E5 | **Crossover entry to rotational knee chamber** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Organize the hip turn after a crossover approach without adding another full kick dose. Take one short crossover travel step and then uncross onto the support foot; rotate with that foot while lifting the free bent knee into a low chamber. Put it down into stance after a 2 s finish. No kick extension or flight; the full entry fits inside 2 m. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: less crossing distance and a lower chamber instead if feet tangle; do not turn while the legs remain crossed. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 5 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Drop-step rotational medicine-ball scoop throw** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Reload the rear hip before delivering the turn. From side-on, move the lead foot slightly away/back to open the stance and settle toward the rear hip; re-establish pressure, pivot and sweep the ball through the low target. Keep the whole action inside 1 m. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Keep all entry and finish footwork inside a cleared 1-metre start bay; no hop, jump or sprint. Use instead: Reduce the step to a small stance opening if the knee twists or balance is lost. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 5 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Side-Lying Dumbbell External Rotation** | **1 × 6 per arm** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly load each shoulder's external-rotation control before the main pressing sets. Lie on side, top elbow at ribs near 90 degrees; very light dumbbell, rotate outward 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without rolling back. Use instead: unanchored bilateral light-band external rotation for 1 × 6 if floor side-lying is unsuitable; record bilateral dosing. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between arm bouts; 12 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| S2 | **Seated Band Ankle Eversion — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light outer-ankle strengthening to resist rolling over the foot edge during the push. Heel supported; hand-held light band draws the forefoot inward. Move forefoot outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s while the knee stays still. Use instead: gentle hand resistance through a smaller range at the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Split Squat — stationary, two dumbbells** | **1 × 4 per leg** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Independent lead-leg knee/hip extension with the rear foot supplying balance. Lower 3 s, rise in about 1 s without changing stance width; front whole foot stays loaded and pelvis stays square. Use instead: a supported split squat with lighter load or bodyweight at a challenging controlled depth. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between leg bouts; 8 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P2 | **One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench** | **2 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the arm-driven backward draw while support prevents torso rotation. Draw elbow toward hip, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; stop before the shoulder rolls forward or the trunk turns. Use instead: a thigh-supported dumbbell row with the same arm path if a bench does not fit. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 60 s between arm bouts; 24 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 2 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow extension for the finish of a forceful press. Neutral grips, upper arms near vertical; lower 2 s beside the ears, extend under control without shoulder swing. Use instead: a lighter floor-supported elbow extension through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 5 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 5 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Split Squat — stationary, two dumbbells:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per leg; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds, 30 s between legs; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 1 cycles; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Seated band hip internal rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 4 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 3 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (6 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 4 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 7 rounds / 11 side-specific bouts, 54 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 2 complete walkout cycles (16 heel placements). Preparation: 31 reps/traverses plus 1 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 22 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 63–70 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted introduces a deliberate shallow leg dip into a guided arm press; Countermovement Jump — hands fixed at hips keeps the hands at the hips so upward leg force remains visible. The independent arm draw-to-release and supported single-leg hinge supply small preload tasks, while the rotational entries add direction through controlled foot placement. Compared with Class 13, prescribed ball releases change from 10 to 8, bilateral foot-landing events change from 4 to 3, airborne feed catches change from 6 to 0. With actual recovery checked, require a single dip-to-press sequence without a second dip and a controlled two-foot jump reception. Use the listed strict landmine speed press for remaining E1 attempts if the transfer is not coordinated, or the listed band press if bar mass is unsuitable. Reduce jump size or use the listed nonflight rise for remaining E2 attempts; preserve resets instead of adding repetitions.

Previous combined Class 13 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 48 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 11 bouts, 54 reps and 2 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_14_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_14.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 15; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 15 of 36

**Stage 5, lesson C: Retained hip return and linked hand push**

**Primary effort:** Accept a lateral arrival into a grounded hip turn and arrest a retained upper-body rotational arc. Linked hand pushes and leg-driven projection support a controlled impulse and finish.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Lateral step-in to rotational plant as the grounded rotational force anchor. Convert arriving lateral bodyweight into a grounded foot-and-hip turn with a controlled split-stance finish.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Lateral step-in to rotational plant. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Lateral step-in to rotational plant and Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop: Retained upper endpoint control paired with familiar lateral step-to-turn. Lower Class 5 jab-step has no explicit turn, so source Class 1 E5 is reused honestly.
- **E3 + E4 — Brief return-to-force link:** Kettlebell Swing — staggered-stance continuous pair and Plyo Push-Up — two linked pairs on elevated hands: Use the controlled return of the retained hip load or hand contact to organize the next impulse, then complete the planned reset.
- **E5 + E6 — Directed implement projection:** Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — upward-forward leg drive and Medicine Ball Overhead Forward Throw — fixed stance, arm-driven: Direct the ball along the declared forward path and keep the body's finish organized; preserve leg drive for the scoop and the arm-led overhead release.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Lateral step-in to rotational plant** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Convert arriving lateral bodyweight into a grounded foot-and-hip turn with a controlled split-stance finish. From a parallel athletic stance, take one brisk short lateral step onto the receiving foot, then turn the feet and pelvis together up to 45 degrees into a shallow split kick stance. Let the receiving heel release and the other foot realign. Hold the finish for 2 seconds. Keep the step and finish within 2 metres, with another 2 metres clear for recovery. No kick, run-up or flight. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: Shorten the step and turn angle if the athlete overstrides, shuffles again or traps the knee. Both feet must be able to realign with the pelvis. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 1 · E5](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_01.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Brake the trunk-and-hands impulse without a release or catch. Hold a very light ball close to the sternum. Make a short fast foot/hip/trunk turn, then stop the ball and body together within a comfortable arc for 2 seconds. Reset slowly; do not whip the ball at long arm length. Use instead: an unloaded palms-together turn instead if ball inertia prevents a clean stop. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 5 · E6](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Kettlebell Swing — staggered-stance continuous pair** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Hip-driven reversal with a lead-biased base and square pelvis. Use a very light bell and shallow stagger, both feet planted. Hike into two continuous chest-height floats without twisting or pushing the bell with the arms, then park. Two swings per lead leg. Use instead: a bilateral continuous swing for the remaining repetitions if stance or bell control fails; record lost asymmetric demand. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E4 | **Plyo Push-Up — two linked pairs on elevated hands** | **1 × 4** | Practice one brief hand-contact reversal before fully resetting. Four push-offs total: perform two small hand-flight reps linked by one soft reception, rest 15 s, then two more. No clap. Use only if prior hand-flight control and first reception are satisfactory; otherwise four separate reps. Use instead: four separated elevated-hand plyo push-ups, resetting 12 s between each, when linked reception is unconfirmed; use non-flight fast presses if hand landing is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** between-rep gaps 0 / 15 / 0 s; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E5 | **Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — upward-forward leg drive** | **1 × 3** | Direct continuous knee/hip extension into an upward rather than long forward release. Use a light ball and one shallow dip; keep arms long as legs drive the ball upward and slightly forward. Feet remain planted. Let the ball land in a cleared contained area; never catch it. Use instead: three rapid unloaded squat-to-calf rises without flight if overhead clearance or containment is unsuitable; log zero throws. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| E6 | **Medicine Ball Overhead Forward Throw — fixed stance, arm-driven** | **1 × 3** | Project from a higher arm position while retaining a forward release line. Light ball, both feet fixed; bring ball to comfortable overhead position, ribs down, throw forward with both arms. No back arch, full-body whip or overhead catch. Use instead: a tall-kneeling chest pass if overhead range cannot stay comfortable, or a self-anchored band speed press if release space is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |

| P1 | **Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |

Use instead: a hands-elevated push-up on an inspected immovable support. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
