Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 5 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_05.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 5 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 5 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_05.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

- **P3 — Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.

Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.

**Workload:** 6 nonrotational and 0 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 0 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (0 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 4 bilateral hand-receptions / 8 hand contacts. 4 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 4 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 6 rounds / 7 side-specific bouts, 42 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 27 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 22 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 55–62 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Lateral step-in to rotational plant repeats the earlier lateral plant so the rotational lesson can emphasize the controlled retained endpoint of Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop. Plyo Push-Up — two linked pairs on elevated hands introduces two linked hand-flight pairs, separated by its explicit 15-second midpoint reset; the staggered swing pair and two forward release paths support force transfer without adding foot landings. Compared with Class 14, prescribed ball releases change from 8 to 6, bilateral foot-landing events change from 3 to 0, bilateral hand-receiving events change from 0 to 4. Require reviewed recovery, a clean rotational stop and individually qualified hand reception. If linked hand control is unconfirmed, use separated elevated-hand repetitions only for the remaining E4 attempts with the listed 12-second resets, or nonflight presses if receiving itself is unsuitable. Use the listed unloaded palms-together turn for remaining E2 attempts if ball inertia prevents the stop; log the changed load and time.

Previous combined Class 14 prescribed 11 primary bouts and 54 conventional/traverse reps plus 2 cycles; this class prescribes 7 bouts, 42 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. This third lesson completes stage coverage and repeats the selected light supports for the stated shoulder/hip/foot jobs. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_15_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_15.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 16; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 16 of 36

**Stage 6, lesson A: Simple starts, smaller dose**

**Primary effort:** Rehearse organized force onset with a deliberately smaller midpoint workload.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Rehearse organized force onset with a deliberately smaller midpoint workload. Consolidate with the reduced planned exposure; previous prescriptions do not establish tolerance.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Familiar settled starts:** Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start and Medicine Ball Chest Pass — still start on a spoken release cue: Use the familiar settled start before the upward jump or forward release, preserving control at the deliberately smaller midpoint dose.
- **E3 + E4 — Stable-base force finish:** Calf Raise — rapid supported rise, no flight and Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause: Keep the support base steady while the ankle or arms complete a short force action; avoid extra body movement to create speed.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Parallel-stance low roundhouse and Unloaded rotational diagonal hand drive: Unloaded diagonal upper action and reduced-dose parallel-start lower turn consolidate at two events each.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Squat Jump — settled quarter-squat start** | **1 × 3** | Main knee/hip force from a still start, expressed vertically. Hold a comfortable quarter squat 2 s with hands at hips; jump vertically without another dip, land on both feet with yielding knees and hold 2 s. Keep jump height within a controlled reception. Use instead: a smaller same-start jump; if flight cannot be controlled, a fast nonflight squat rise at the same dose and zero landings. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E2 | **Medicine Ball Chest Pass — still start on a spoken release cue** | **2 × 2** | Express forward force promptly from an organized still position without pre-tensing into a dip. Hold ball at chest, feet fixed. Coach gives one simple spoken cue after a comfortable unpredictable 2–4 s wait; release straight ahead. No direction choice or extra reaction drill. Use instead: a self-initiated stationary chest pass if cue timing disrupts posture; keep four total attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E3 | **Calf Raise — rapid supported rise, no flight** | **1 × 3** | Express a quick ankle finish while keeping forefeet continuously grounded. With stable hand support and soft-straight knees, rise onto forefeet quickly, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to floor. No jump or bouncing heel drop. Use instead: a smaller unloaded quick heel rise with the same grounded dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E4 | **Two-Hand Landmine Bent-Over Row — fast pull from a pause** | **1 × 3** | Recheck fast arm-driven pulling at a small dose without new grip or support demands. Light sleeve grip, still hinge; quick pull, controlled 2 s return and still reset. No hip extension. Use instead: a light two-hand kettlebell paused row if landmine handling is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 4 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E5 | **Parallel-stance low roundhouse** | **1 × 1 per side/direction** | Organize support selection and the hip turn from a neutral two-foot base. Start with both feet parallel in a comfortable athletic stance. Shift onto the support leg and turn its foot/pelvis into one low roundhouse with the other leg; recoil and return to the original parallel base. No preselected front/rear fighting stance or entry step. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: Widen the starting base slightly or reduce the kick arc instead if the athlete needs a rescue step; feet must be free to turn. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 6 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Unloaded rotational diagonal hand drive** | **1 × 1 per side/direction** | Link a short body turn to a downward single-arm finish. No target or implement. Start the open hand near the opposite upper-chest line; pivot and drive it diagonally toward the same-side front pocket with a softly bent elbow. Stop clear of the thigh and reset slowly. Use instead: Shorten the path if the shoulder drops or the trunk bends sideways. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 6 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Scapular Push-Up — quadruped, dynamic** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly load controlled shoulder-blade travel for pushing support and force transfer. Hands and knees down; keep elbows straight. Settle chest 2 s, push away 2 s, hold 2 s without trunk sag. Use instead: a smaller controlled scapular range with more weight supported at knees. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| S2 | **Tibialis Raise — wall-supported** | **1 × 4** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light ankle dorsiflexor loading to control foot position before a stable push/landing. Back against an inspected wall, heels on floor; lift forefeet 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Adjust foot distance to stay easy. Use instead: a seated unweighted toe raise through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Goblet Squat — dumbbell** | **1 × 4** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Bilateral knee/hip extension through a repeatable full-foot push. Lower 3 s, stand decisively in about 1 s; hold a manageable goblet load and retain comfortable depth without heels lifting. Use instead: a lighter goblet squat through controlled comfortable range; a two-dumbbell front hold may replace a limiting goblet grip. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P2 | **Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main horizontal pressing force with a reproducible floor-limited range. Lower 2 s, touch upper arms gently, pause 1 s, then press decisively; feet stay down. Coach supervises pickup/set-down. Use instead: a lighter floor press through controlled comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P3 | **Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force with the knees extended for the final upward/forward push. Use stable hand support if needed; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to the floor. Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Strengthen the wider-elbow backward draw that controls the arm-open position. Hold a still hinge; elbows about 60 degrees from torso, pull to upper ribs, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Use instead: a chest-supported version only on an inspected bench with adequate arm clearance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 4 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 8 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 6 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Cable / Band Chop — standing band execution** | **1 × 4 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the downward diagonal arm path and its controlled return, supporting rotational hand acceleration and braking without adding another release. Secure a band at a verified high anchor to one side. Stand in a comfortable split stance with the opposite foot forward. With both hands, move from above the anchor-side shoulder toward the opposite front pocket while feet and hips turn through a modest arc. Drive in 1–2 seconds and return in 3 seconds. Keep the trunk long; avoid sideways crunching and band recoil. No cable machine is assumed. Use instead: Reduce band resistance or diagonal range if the athlete side-bends or loses control. If a suitable secure high anchor is unavailable, stop and re-audit the slot; do not improvise an unsafe anchor. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 8 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 6 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

Review actual recovery and require the same organized jump start plus a chest-pass posture that survives the short cue wait. If the cue disrupts control, use a self-initiated stationary pass only for remaining E2 attempts; if flight is unqualified, use the listed nonflight squat rise for remaining E1 work. Keep full resets despite the smaller dose and add no comparison throws.

Previous combined Class 15 prescribed 7 primary bouts and 42 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 8 bouts, 37 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Stage 6 deliberately retains the reduced force prescriptions and reduces rotation to one attempt per side and four working reps per side/traverse. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_16_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_16.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 17; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 17 of 36

**Stage 6, lesson B: Grounded hip and gripping force**

**Primary effort:** Consolidate a grounded hip impulse and a short controlled arm-force transfer.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Supine Dumbbell Serratus Reach — brief rapid finish as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Consolidate a grounded hip impulse and a short controlled arm-force transfer. Consolidate with the reduced planned exposure; previous prescriptions do not establish tolerance.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Supine Dumbbell Serratus Reach — brief rapid finish. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Supported force without flight:** Supine Dumbbell Serratus Reach — brief rapid finish and Kettlebell Swing — dead-stop two-hand repetitions: Use the floor support to stabilize the body while the shoulder blades or hips produce the brief impulse, keeping the endpoint controlled.
- **E3 + E4 — Controlled traction and hip finish:** Seated Hand-over-Hand Battle-Rope Pull — free rope on floor and Glute Bridge — fast grounded rise, settled floor start: Keep the supported torso organized while the arms transfer traction or the hips rise; finish without a backward lean or back arch.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Supported low outward crescent sweep and Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop: Retained upper turn and supported outward low-leg sweep reduce release/landing exposure.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Supine Dumbbell Serratus Reach — brief rapid finish** | **1 × 4** | Express the final scapular reach of a push while the floor supports the trunk. Very light dumbbells held above chest, elbows remain straight. Reach shoulder blades up a small amount quickly, return 2 s; no shrug, throw or elbow bend. Use instead: an unloaded supine rapid reach if the implement produces shoulder strain or elbow substitution. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 4 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E2 | **Kettlebell Swing — dead-stop two-hand repetitions** | **1 × 3** | Rapid hip extension moves a retained implement without adding landings. Hike a light kettlebell from a stable parked start, snap hips to a tall finish with a chest-level float, guide the return and park fully after each rep. No squat pull, arm lift or lumbar arch. Use instead: a very light rapid dumbbell hip hinge with a 2 s lowering and still finish if swing skill or grip is unconfirmed. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E3 | **Seated Hand-over-Hand Battle-Rope Pull — free rope on floor** | **1 × 4** | Retain short grip-transfer traction without a long continuous pulling interval. Use the inspected free-rope setup from Class 2; alternate two short pulls per arm with torso still and no rope around the body. Use instead: an alternating light seated band row, two pulls per arm, if rope mass or friction is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 3 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E4 | **Glute Bridge — fast grounded rise, settled floor start** | **1 × 3** | Express hip extension near its finish without another lengthened hinge or landing. Bodyweight/light secured pelvic load; settle hips gently on floor, drive up quickly, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Keep feet down and knees still; finish without back arch. Use instead: an unloaded smaller-range fast bridge if load handling or pelvic control is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E5 | **Supported low outward crescent sweep** | **1 × 1 per side/direction** | Express a small outward hip sweep and actively arrest and recover the free leg under control. With light fingertip support, start the low free foot near the midline and sweep it outward in a small arc with a soft knee and comfortable hip turn, then fold the knee and set down. No high lift or forced crossing start. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a bent-knee outward sweep instead if the longer lever causes groin pull or pelvic lean; do not rotate against a fixed support foot. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 6 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop** | **1 × 1 per side/direction** | Review active braking of body and implement together. Hold a very light ball at the sternum. Make a short fast pivot/turn and stop the ball and body together for 2 seconds; reset without a release. Use instead: an unloaded palms-together turn instead if inertia overwhelms the endpoint. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 6 · E5](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Bilateral Band External Rotation — elbows at sides** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light shoulder-rotator strengthening to control upper-arm position during the day's press and pull. Unanchored band between hands, elbows at ribs; rotate outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s without shrugging. Use instead: a lower-tension band and shorter comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| S2 | **Side-Lying Hip Abduction — light** | **1 × 4 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light lateral-hip strengthening to keep the pelvis and knee organized over the force-producing leg. Lie on side, lower leg bent; lift upper leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s with toes facing forward and pelvis still. Bodyweight is sufficient unless clearly easy. Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 8 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells** | **1 × 4** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P2 | **One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench** | **1 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the arm-driven backward draw while support prevents torso rotation. Draw elbow toward hip, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; stop before the shoulder rolls forward or the trunk turns. Use instead: a thigh-supported dumbbell row with the same arm path if a bench does not fit. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 12 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 2 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow extension for the finish of a forceful press. Neutral grips, upper arms near vertical; lower 2 s beside the ears, extend under control without shoulder swing. Use instead: a lighter floor-supported elbow extension through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 4 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 8 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 6 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 4 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 6 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 1 cycles; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Loaded Cossack Squat:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 0 nonrotational and 0 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 0 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (0 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 6 grounded rapid leg reps, 2 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 2 kick actions) and 2 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 6 rounds / 8 side-specific bouts, 34 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 2 complete walkout cycles (16 heel placements). Preparation: 28 reps/traverses plus 1 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 14 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 53–60 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Supine Dumbbell Serratus Reach — brief rapid finish gives the supine shoulder-blade finish a brief rapid task, supported by familiar parked hip drive and a reduced four-total-draw rope task. Both rotational entries remain small and retained or unloaded, keeping this midpoint lesson grounded without free ball release or hand/foot reception. Compared with Class 16, prescribed ball releases change from 4 to 0, bilateral foot-landing events change from 3 to 0, explosive set/side bouts change from 9 to 8. Delivery remains conditional on actual recovery and a shoulder-blade action without elbow substitution or strain. Use the listed unloaded supine rapid reach for remaining E1 attempts if the dumbbell disrupts the action, and the listed light rapid dumbbell hinge for remaining E2 attempts if swing skill or grip is unqualified. Preserve the side rests for the supported sweep and stop-to-turn work.

Previous combined Class 16 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 37 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 8 bouts, 34 reps and 2 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Stage 6 deliberately retains the reduced force prescriptions and reduces rotation to one attempt per side and four working reps per side/traverse. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. ## Full Body Force Generation — Class 18 of 36

**Stage 6, lesson C: Low-dose lead-leg and release**

**Primary effort:** Consolidate direct hip external-rotation force and retained rotational pulling at the reduced planned dose. Small lead-leg and linear arm-force tasks support an organized base and finish.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Seated self-resisted hip external-rotation acceleration as the grounded rotational force anchor. Give the return-direction hip rotators a small rapid torque task with standing balance removed. Consolidate with the reduced planned exposure; previous prescriptions do not establish tolerance.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Seated self-resisted hip external-rotation acceleration. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Seated self-resisted hip external-rotation acceleration and Rotational band row speed drive: Predictable upper pulling turn and seated direct hip external-rotation acceleration preserve rotational work with no flight or release.
- **E3 + E4 — Low-dose support and finish:** Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins and Band Chest Press — simultaneous fast extension, no release: Maintain the declared support position while the lead leg rises or the arms press, completing each small force action without extra travel.
- **E5 + E6 — Small projection with a balanced finish:** Countermovement Jump — hands fixed at hips and Medicine Ball Shot Put Throw — forward-facing, no rotation: Express the compact vertical or forward impulse from the declared base and finish organized, keeping the independent arm release free of an added turn.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Seated self-resisted hip external-rotation acceleration** | **1 × 1 per side/direction** | Give the return-direction hip rotators a small rapid torque task with standing balance removed. Sit with thighs supported and knee near 90 degrees. The working foot moves a short distance inward as the hip externally rotates; the same-side hand gives very light opposing pressure at the inner lower shin. Accelerate smoothly, then return slowly with knee/pelvis still. Use instead: Reduce pressure/range instead for any knee/ankle motion or pinching; never jerk into end range. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 6 · E6](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Rotational band row speed drive** | **1 × 1 per side/direction** | Review a fast pulling contribution with controlled return. Light band ahead at lower-chest height. From a small reach, pivot and draw the elbow toward lower ribs quickly, then return in 3 seconds without shrugging. Verify the stated band anchor and tension; retain the band throughout, with no release or uncontrolled recoil. Use instead: Reduce tension/reach if the trunk yanks or shoulder hikes. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 6 · E6](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins** | **1 × 1 per leg** | Let one lead leg supply upward force against a stable support without hand-off to a jump. Use a low inspected immovable step. Front whole foot planted; rise quickly without springing from the trailing foot, bring that foot onto the step, then descend under control in 3 s. No flight. Use instead: a supported fast split-squat rise with both feet grounded and the same per-leg dose if support fit fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E4 | **Band Chest Press — simultaneous fast extension, no release** | **1 × 3** | Check a quick, controlled press finish without adding throws or hand impacts. Permitted band around upper back below neck, secure hands; fast forward extension, controlled 2 s return, feet fixed. Use instead: a non-flight fast incline push-up if the self-anchored band cannot be secured. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| E5 | **Countermovement Jump — hands fixed at hips** | **1 × 2** | Express upward knee/hip force without an arm swing masking the leg strategy. Keep hands at hips, make one comfortable dip and jump vertically; land on both feet and hold 2 s. Do not compare height with arm-assisted jumps. Use instead: a smaller hands-at-hips vertical jump or nonflight rapid squat rise at the same remaining dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |

Use instead: a self-anchored single-arm band speed press if one-hand ball control or containment is unsuitable; record the missing release work. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |

| S1 | **Scapular Push-Up — quadruped, dynamic** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly load controlled shoulder-blade travel for pushing support and force transfer. Hands and knees down; keep elbows straight. Settle chest 2 s, push away 2 s, hold 2 s without trunk sag. Use instead: a smaller controlled scapular range with more weight supported at knees. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |

Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose.
