### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported** | **1 × 4** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension near the finish with the knee angle held, without another lengthened-hinge dose. Secure a manageable load over the pelvis; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Finish with ribs/pelvis aligned, not lumbar arching. Use instead: a lighter loaded floor bridge with the same controlled range; a shorter lever may be used if hamstrings cramp. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P2 | **Push-Up — controlled full repetition** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the press with free shoulder-blade movement and a steady trunk. Lower 2 s, push decisively, finish tall through shoulders for 1 s; no hand flight. Adjust leverage or band resistance to reach 2–3 RIR. Use instead: a hands-elevated push-up on an inspected immovable support. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P3 | **Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force while the knee remains flexed during early push and receiving. Sit on an inspected stable bench, feet on floor; secure loads on thighs, rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without knee-angle change or bouncing. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee calf raise with the same bilateral dose and 2–3 technical RIR if bench/load support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 6 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_06.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 4 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 8 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 6 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 4 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 6 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_06.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Push-Up — controlled full repetition:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 3. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Loaded Cossack Squat:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 4 nonrotational and 0 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 2 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (4 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 2 grounded rapid leg reps, 2 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 2 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 6 rounds / 7 side-specific bouts, 33 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 24 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 14 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 51–58 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Seated self-resisted hip external-rotation acceleration changes the direct seated hip job to self-resisted external-rotation acceleration, paired with a familiar pulling turn in Rotational band row speed drive; both retain one attempt per side. The two small hands-fixed jumps and four independent forward releases supply support without restoring the earlier full anchor doses. Compared with Class 17, prescribed ball releases change from 0 to 4, bilateral foot-landing events change from 0 to 2, explosive set/side bouts change from 8 to 10. Confirm actual recovery and require hip rotation without knee/ankle motion and a pulling turn without a trunk yank. Reduce only the remaining seated range or hand resistance if those controls fail; reduce band tension or reach for remaining rotational rows. Manual resistance is less reproducible, so record it explicitly and do not interpret apparent speed across different resistance as improvement.

Previous combined Class 17 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 34 conventional/traverse reps plus 2 cycles; this class prescribes 7 bouts, 33 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Stage 6 deliberately retains the reduced force prescriptions and reduces rotation to one attempt per side and four working reps per side/traverse. This third lesson completes stage coverage and repeats the selected light supports for the stated shoulder/hip/foot jobs. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_18_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_18.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 19; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 19 of 36

**Stage 7, lesson A: Independent takeoff and received return**

**Primary effort:** Express force through one takeoff leg and one returning arm while keeping reception controlled.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Single-Leg Vertical Takeoff — two-foot floor reception as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Express force through one takeoff leg and one returning arm while keeping reception controlled.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Single-Leg Vertical Takeoff — two-foot floor reception. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Independent impulse and reception:** Single-Leg Vertical Takeoff — two-foot floor reception and Medicine Ball Two-Hand Receive to Single-Arm Forward Return: Organize force through one takeoff leg or one returning arm, then control the two-foot landing or the body's release finish.
- **E3 + E4 — Stable support for an independent finish:** Bent-Knee Calf Raise — single-leg rapid supported rise and One-Arm Row — supported dumbbell speed pull: Keep the supported trunk and force-producing side organized while the calf or arm completes its draw, without adding a pelvic roll.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Lateral hip rock-to-pivot release and Externally supplied rotational scoop return: Controlled coach handoff adds supplied-ball organization; lower rock-to-pivot adds a small controlled reversal without a bounce.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Single-Leg Vertical Takeoff — two-foot floor reception** | **1 × 3 per leg** | Main independent stance-leg upward push with a deliberately broader receiving base. Stand on one leg, make a small controlled dip and project a modest height; bring both feet under the pelvis and receive on two feet, holding 2 s. Three attempts per takeoff leg. No extra preparatory hop. Use instead: two bouts of three bilateral settled-start squat jumps with hands at hips, 10 s between reps and 60 s between bouts, then the same 90 s transition; hold the start 2 s without a second dip and receive on both feet for 2 s. This replaces unilateral takeoffs with bilateral takeoffs and differs from the source exercise “Single-Leg Squat Rise — supported rapid quarter-range” grounded single-leg force. If bilateral receiving also fails, stop the remaining flight work and record a modified session. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between leg bouts; 6 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E2 | **Medicine Ball Two-Hand Receive to Single-Arm Forward Return** | **1 × 3 per arm** | Primary transfer from a symmetric received load to an independent arm release while staying square. Coach gives a gentle chest-height feed; receive with both hands, settle briefly at the selected shoulder, remove assisting hand and push forward. Complete one arm's reps then change sides; no rotational wind-up. Use instead: a self-started stationary single-arm shot-put throw when receiving quality or ball control is unconfirmed; use a secured single-arm band press if release setup fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 6 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E3 | **Bent-Knee Calf Raise — single-leg rapid supported rise** | **1 × 3 per leg** | Produce quick stance-ankle force while holding a modest knee bend. Use stable hand support, keep one foot on the floor and knee softly bent; rise quickly onto the forefoot, hold 1 s and lower 2 s. Forefoot stays grounded. Use instead: an assisted bilateral bent-knee rapid raise for the same set/rep count if needed; record changed laterality. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 6 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E4 | **One-Arm Row — supported dumbbell speed pull** | **1 × 3 per arm** | Express the backward draw independently without opposite-arm assistance. Hand and knee supported on inspected bench; light dumbbell, quick elbow-to-hip pull, lower 2 s, still reset; no torso rotation. Use instead: a thigh-supported light dumbbell row with the same side dose if bench fit is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 6 total reps; 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E5 | **Lateral hip rock-to-pivot release** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Express force after a small continuous lateral preload without another landing. With both feet initially grounded in a comfortable stance, shift a short distance into the named hip and immediately push out of that load into a small hip turn. Let the feet turn with the pelvis and finish in kick stance; no step-in or jump. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: a smaller rock or a brief pause instead if the turn becomes a lumbar twist or foot pressure is lost. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 7 · E3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Externally supplied rotational scoop return** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Organize a supplied ball into a balanced rotational release. While the athlete is stationary, the coach places a light ball securely into both hands near the rear hip. After the coach fully releases and clears the motion and release path, establish grip, turn feet, hips and trunk, then make a low forward scoop release. This combined execution uses a controlled handoff only, with no airborne feed or rebound catch. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: a self-held paused rotational scoop instead if a safe handoff, secure grip, feeder clearance or balanced turn cannot be qualified; this replacement has no ball reception. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 7 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

Independent lead-leg knee/hip extension with the rear foot supplying balance. Lower 3 s, rise in about 1 s without changing stance width; front whole foot stays loaded and pelvis stays square. Use instead: a supported split squat with lighter load or bodyweight at a challenging controlled depth. Free arm rests on floor for balance without pushing; keep feet down. Lower 2 s, gentle floor pause 1 s, press decisively without rotating. Use instead: a lighter floor press through controlled comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 150 s between complete set rounds; 60 s between arm bouts; 20 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |

Independent stance-ankle force without the other leg masking heel travel. Hold stable support, rise 1 s, hold 1 s and lower 2 s to floor; keep knee softly straight and heel aligned. Scale load and hand assistance for reserve. Use instead: an assisted single-leg raise at the same per-leg dose; if changed to bilateral, use the same set/rep count and record lost unilateral loading. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Strengthen the wider-elbow backward draw that controls the arm-open position. Hold a still hinge; elbows about 60 degrees from torso, pull to upper ribs, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Use instead: a chest-supported version only on an inspected bench with adequate arm clearance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 7 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Cable / Band Lift — standing band execution** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the low-to-high turning path from the hip to the hands and control its return, supporting the selected rising or scooping upper rotational action. Secure a band at a verified low anchor behind one side. Stand in a comfortable split stance. Guide both hands from the rear hip across toward the opposite upper chest, allowing a small foot-and-hip turn. Drive in 1–2 seconds and return in 3 seconds. Keep ribs stacked and stop below any range that requires leaning back or shrugging. No cable machine is assumed. Use instead: Reduce resistance or range if the athlete leans back or loses a controlled return. If a suitable secure low anchor is unavailable, stop and re-audit the slot; do not improvise an unsafe anchor. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 7 · P5](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. **Progression note:** Single-Leg Vertical Takeoff — two-foot floor reception changes vertical projection to one-leg takeoff while retaining two-foot reception. This post-consolidation prescription introduces these constraints without evidence that the athlete has earned them. Review actual recovery and qualify each support and receiving task separately: level takeoff, controlled two-foot reception, secure single-arm ball control and feeder clearance before turning. Use the listed bilateral settled-start jumps for remaining E1 attempts when single-leg takeoff is unqualified; use self-started single-arm throws for remaining E2 attempts and self-held paused scoops for remaining E6 attempts when their receiving arrangements fail. Record changed takeoffs, catches and handoffs; do not add retries.

Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 20 of 36

**Stage 7, lesson B: Supported arm and staggered hip force**

**Primary effort:** Generate independent arm force and a staggered hip impulse from controlled support.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Floor Press — single-arm light dumbbell speed press as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Generate independent arm force and a staggered hip impulse from controlled support.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Floor Press — single-arm light dumbbell speed press. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Independent arm force and hip initiation:** Floor Press — single-arm light dumbbell speed press and Kettlebell Swing — staggered dead-stop repetitions: Set the support and load before the impulse; retain trunk control through the independent press and the staggered hip-driven swing.
- **E3 + E4 — One-side force with a level base:** Band Lat Pulldown — single-arm rapid elbow drive and Glute Bridge — single-leg rapid rise with level pelvis: Keep the pelvis and rib cage organized as one arm draws or one hip rises, finishing without a lateral trunk bend.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Bent-knee counter-sweep to chamber and Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop: Familiar upper snap-to-stop and short bent-knee lower counter-sweep emphasize rapid turn with a controlled endpoint.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Floor Press — single-arm light dumbbell speed press** | **1 × 3 per arm** | Accelerate an independent pressing load while the trunk resists rolling. Supine, feet fixed and free arm resting on floor; upper arm touches gently, then press quickly without releasing the dumbbell; lower 2 s. Use instead: a self-anchored single-arm band speed press with the nonworking hand securing the tail if floor/load handling is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 6 total reps; 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E2 | **Kettlebell Swing — staggered dead-stop repetitions** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Start hip-driven force from a parked bell with a lead-biased but two-foot base. Use a shallow stagger and very light bell, hips/pelvis square. Hike, drive one chest-height float and park; reset before the next rep. Complete two per lead leg, without arm lifting or twisting. Use instead: a supported single-leg rapid hinge return at the same per-leg dose, with no bell, if parking control or minimum bell load is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E3 | **Band Lat Pulldown — single-arm rapid elbow drive** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Keep independent overhead pulling with the ribs held level. Rated overhead attachment, tall kneeling base; quick elbow-to-rib draw, controlled 2 s return, no side bend. Use instead: a single-arm supported dumbbell speed row if no suitable overhead anchor exists; record the vertical-pull gap. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E4 | **Glute Bridge — single-leg rapid rise with level pelvis** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Express hip extension on each side without the second leg hiding pelvic control. One foot grounded, other thigh comfortably lifted; drive hips up quickly, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without twist. Use bodyweight and a short controllable range. Use instead: a kickstand-assisted rapid bridge at the same per-leg dose if the pelvis rotates; record assistance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E5 | **Bent-knee counter-sweep to chamber** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Coordinate a short free-thigh loading reversal with hip-turn initiation. Hold the free knee folded below hip height, move the thigh a small distance outward/back under control, then reverse briskly into a low forward roundhouse chamber as the support foot releases. No knee extension or ballistic end-range stretch. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: a smaller counter-sweep or a still start instead if the pelvis tips or the knee is flung beyond control. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 7 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Close-held medicine-ball rotational snap-to-stop** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Check body-and-ball endpoint control without another incoming ball. Hold the light ball close to the sternum, make a compact fast pivot/turn, and hold the endpoint for 2 seconds. Reset slowly. Use instead: an unloaded turn instead if the load cannot be stopped cleanly. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 7 · E5](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |

Light shoulder-rotator strengthening to control upper-arm position during the day's press and pull. Unanchored band between hands, elbows at ribs; rotate outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s without shrugging. Use instead: a lower-tension band and shorter comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| S2 | **Seated Band Ankle Inversion — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light inner-ankle strengthening to organize the foot arch without toe clawing. Heel supported; hand-held light band draws the forefoot outward. Move forefoot inward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s with shin still. Use instead: gentle hand resistance through a smaller range at the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| P1 | **Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P2 | **One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench** | **1 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the arm-driven backward draw while support prevents torso rotation. Draw elbow toward hip, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; stop before the shoulder rolls forward or the trunk turns. Use instead: a thigh-supported dumbbell row with the same arm path if a bench does not fit. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 12 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 2 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow extension for the finish of a forceful press. Neutral grips, upper arms near vertical; lower 2 s beside the ears, extend under control without shoulder swing. Use instead: a lighter floor-supported elbow extension through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 7 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 7 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

Keep the full per-side recoveries; twelve explosive bouts remain twelve separate support conditions, not a circuit.

Previous combined Class 19 prescribed 13 primary bouts and 66 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 8 bouts, 39 reps and 2 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. ## Full Body Force Generation — Class 21 of 36

**Stage 7, lesson C: Single-leg control with guided projection**

**Primary effort:** Reorganize a grounded stance into rotational knee drive and a controlled upper-body hand sweep. Single-leg support and guided or free forward pressing reinforce force transfer.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Grounded switch-step rotational knee drive as the grounded rotational force anchor. Link a fast support-role exchange to proximal rotational output without a full additional kick.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Grounded switch-step rotational knee drive. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Grounded switch-step rotational knee drive and Two-hand band backhand speed sweep: Predictable retained upper sweep and grounded lower stance exchange preserve rotation while avoiding linked-jump volume.
- **E3 + E4 — Supported knee drive and guided projection:** Single-Leg Squat Rise — supported rapid quarter-range and Landmine Push Press — single arm, shallow dip: Keep pressure through the supporting leg as force begins; retain the squat rise's support and the press's deliberate shallow dip-to-arm sequence.
- **E5 + E6 — Small projection from organized support:** Single-Leg Forward Takeoff — two-foot floor reception and Medicine Ball Chest Pass — stationary, paused: Direct the single-leg forward impulse or stationary hand release from a controlled base, then finish balanced without adding a run-up.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Grounded switch-step rotational knee drive** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Link a fast support-role exchange to proximal rotational output without a full additional kick. Exchange front/rear foot positions with two short grounded steps, let the new support foot settle and turn, and drive the free folded knee across into a low rotational chamber. No hop, knee extension or contact; finish within 2 m. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: slower, shorter exchange steps instead until the legs uncross cleanly; never pivot while the feet are tangled. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 7 · E5](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Two-hand band backhand speed sweep** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Apply a predictable resisted sweep after the externally supplied tasks. Band anchored at chest height to the start side. Sweep both hands across a small transverse arc as feet/hips turn, stopping cleanly and returning slowly. Verify the stated band anchor and tension; retain the band throughout, with no release or uncontrolled recoil. Use instead: Reduce tension/arc if the body leans or the band recoils. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 7 · E6](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Single-Leg Squat Rise — supported rapid quarter-range** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Express stance-leg knee extension without flight or a rear foot contributing. Hold stable support, free foot clear; lower to a shallow controlled squat for 2 s, hold 1 s and rise quickly through the whole foot. Use the hand for balance/needed assistance, not a sudden pull. Use instead: a supported split-squat rapid rise at the same per-leg dose when the single-leg position cannot stay organized. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E4 | **Landmine Push Press — single arm, shallow dip** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Coordinate a small shared leg preload into one arm's forward/upward drive without turning. Feet stay fixed, short knee dip; extend legs then finish with one arm. Keep sleeve in hand, lower 2 s; no jump or re-bend catch. Use instead: the strict single-arm speed press if dip-to-arm timing or torso control is not demonstrated; use band pressing if bar fit fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 8 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E5 | **Single-Leg Forward Takeoff — two-foot floor reception** | **1 × 1 per leg** | Apply the independent leg push to a short forward rather than upward projection. One modest forward takeoff per leg from a quiet single-leg stance; use a small dip, project into the marked receiving zone and bring both feet to a controlled 2 s reception. No distance target or extra hop. Use instead: one supported fast step-up per leg without flight, using the same 60 s side recovery, if a forward reception cannot fit or stay controlled. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between leg bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| E6 | **Medicine Ball Chest Pass — stationary, paused** | **1 × 2** | Finish with two symmetric releases to check that the athlete can reconnect both arms to the same forward line. Fixed parallel feet, still 1 s chest hold, simultaneous release without dip or twist. Use instead: a self-anchored bilateral band speed press if containment is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Bilateral Band External Rotation — elbows at sides** | **1 × 8** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light shoulder-rotator strengthening to control upper-arm position during the day's press and pull. Unanchored band between hands, elbows at ribs; rotate outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s without shrugging. Use instead: a lower-tension band and shorter comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| S2 | **Side-Lying Hip Abduction — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light lateral-hip strengthening to keep the pelvis and knee organized over the force-producing leg. Lie on side, lower leg bent; lift upper leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s with toes facing forward and pelvis still. Bodyweight is sufficient unless clearly easy. Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension near the finish with the knee angle held, without another lengthened-hinge dose. Secure a manageable load over the pelvis; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Finish with ribs/pelvis aligned, not lumbar arching. Use instead: a lighter loaded floor bridge with the same controlled range; a shorter lever may be used if hamstrings cramp. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P2 | **Landmine Press — strict single arm** | **1 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the independent forward/upward arm drive with a stable torso. Feet fixed, bar at shoulder; no dip, press under control, lower 2 s without side bend or rib flare. Use instead: a single-arm self-anchored band chest press if the minimum bar load or grip is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 12 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P3 | **Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force while the knee remains flexed during early push and receiving. Sit on an inspected stable bench, feet on floor; secure loads on thighs, rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without knee-angle change or bouncing. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee calf raise with the same bilateral dose and 2–3 technical RIR if bench/load support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 7 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_07.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 7 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 7 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_07.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Landmine Press — strict single arm:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Loaded Cossack Squat:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 2 nonrotational and 0 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 2 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (4 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 2 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 4 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 4 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 6 rounds / 8 side-specific bouts, 45 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 33 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 18 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 57–64 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Grounded switch-step rotational knee drive introduces a grounded stance exchange into rotational knee drive, while the familiar sweep of Two-hand band backhand speed sweep keeps the upper rotational path predictable. Single-Leg Forward Takeoff — two-foot floor reception applies the independent leg impulse forward with only one attempt per leg and retains two-foot reception; the guided press and small stationary chest release support the force finish. Compared with Class 20, prescribed ball releases change from 0 to 2, bilateral foot-landing events change from 0 to 2, explosive set/side bouts change from 12 to 11. Require reviewed recovery, feet that uncross before pivoting and a controlled forward reception within the marked zone. Slow or shorten only the remaining stance exchanges if foot organization fails; if only slow rehearsal is qualified, record technique work. Use the listed supported fast step-up for each remaining forward-jump attempt when receiving or space is unsuitable, preserving side rest and recording zero replacement landings.

Previous combined Class 20 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 39 conventional/traverse reps plus 2 cycles; this class prescribes 8 bouts, 45 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. This third lesson completes stage coverage and repeats the selected light supports for the stated shoulder/hip/foot jobs. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown.
