## Full Body Force Generation — Class 22 of 36

**Stage 8, lesson A: Hip-to-release and shoulder pull**

**Primary effort:** Transmit hip force into a release or draw, with a controlled ankle and shoulder finish.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — held hinge to forefoot finish as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Transmit hip force into a release or draw, with a controlled ankle and shoulder finish.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — held hinge to forefoot finish. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Hip-to-implement force transfer:** Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — held hinge to forefoot finish and Landmine Power Row — continuous hip-to-arm drive: Organize the hip impulse so it continues into the forward scoop or arm draw, finishing tall without an added lumbar arch.
- **E3 + E4 — Controlled ankle and shoulder finish:** Seated Bent-Knee Calf Raise — fast light rise and Medicine Ball Overhead Slam — single arm, no trunk rotation: Keep the support base organized while the ankle rises or shoulder drives the ball downward, preserving each task's declared line.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Forward step-in low roundhouse and Tall-kneeling diagonal medicine-ball side slam: Constrained-base upper diagonal slam and lower forward step-in kick separate trunk/hand expression from stance-leg arrival.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — held hinge to forefoot finish** | **2 × 3** | Main hip-to-ankle sequence transferring leg force into an upward-forward release. Hold a comfortable hinge for 2 s with long arms and a light ball. Drive hips forward and rise onto forefeet as the ball floats upward-forward; keep forefeet grounded and do not add an arm curl, second dip or lumbar arch. Let the ball land, never catch. Use instead: a light-dumbbell held-hinge to rapid tall rise with feet flat, six total reps in the same 2 × 3 structure and 90 s set recovery; log no throws and no ankle finish if omitted. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E2 | **Landmine Power Row — continuous hip-to-arm drive** | **2 × 3** | Primary transfer of a small body impulse through the trunk into a forceful backward arm draw. Light two-hand sleeve grip; start in a short hinge. Extend hips slightly as elbows draw toward lower ribs in one connected pull, then lower/reset under control. No separate deadlift rep, shrug, jump, rotation or bar release. Use instead: the paused arm-driven landmine row if hip-to-arm timing is not controlled, or a light two-hand kettlebell row if bar fit fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 8 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E3 | **Seated Bent-Knee Calf Raise — fast light rise** | **1 × 3** | Express rapid plantar-flexion force with the knee held flexed. Sit securely, feet on floor, very light controlled thigh loads if needed; lift heels quickly, hold 1 s and lower 2 s. Keep knee angle fixed; no bouncing or hand pull on the loads. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee rapid heel rise at the same bilateral dose if the seated setup fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E4 | **Medicine Ball Overhead Slam — single arm, no trunk rotation** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Express each shoulder's downward force independently while remaining square. Use a small light low-rebound ball that one hand controls; raise to comfortable overhead reach, pull down to a floor target in front of that side. Feet planted, no crunch or twist, no catching the rebound. Use instead: four two-hand arm-driven slams total if one-arm ball fit or overhead control is unsuitable; if the floor is unsuitable use a single-arm rapid band pulldown and log zero releases. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E5 | **Forward step-in low roundhouse** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Directly translate controlled forward arrival into pelvic rotation and free-leg acceleration. With the kicking leg initially rear, step the support foot a short distance forward, accept weight with a soft knee, let that foot turn and launch the low rear-leg roundhouse. Recoil and recover within the same 2 m work segment. No run-up or pad. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a shorter/slower step instead for overstriding or extra braking steps; preserve controlled brisk kick intent after the qualified plant. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. Start a light ball above the forehead, make a small comfortable thoracic turn and drive it to the floor slightly forward/outside one knee; do not twist farther by fixing the pelvis rigidly. Reset fully. Use a verified non-rebounding, impact-compatible light slam ball and a permitted contained floor impact area; wait for the ball to stop before retrieval. No rebound catch. Use instead: the standing side-slam replacement if kneeling contact is intolerable; shorten overhead reach if ribs flare. ### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |

Priority horizontal pulling strength: move the load through the shoulder and elbow while keeping the body still. Draw elbow toward hip, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; stop before the shoulder rolls forward or the trunk turns. Use instead: a thigh-supported dumbbell row with the same arm path if a bench does not fit. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 60 s between arm bouts; 24 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |

Plantar-flexion force with the knees extended for the final upward/forward push. Use stable hand support if needed; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to the floor. Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow extension for the finish of a forceful press. Neutral grips, upper arms near vertical; lower 2 s beside the ears, extend under control without shoulder swing. Use instead: a lighter floor-supported elbow extension through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |

Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 8 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Load the downward diagonal arm path and its controlled return, supporting rotational hand acceleration and braking without adding another release. Secure a band at a verified high anchor to one side. Stand in a comfortable split stance with the opposite foot forward. With both hands, move from above the anchor-side shoulder toward the opposite front pocket while feet and hips turn through a modest arc. Drive in 1–2 seconds and return in 3 seconds. Keep the trunk long; avoid sideways crunching and band recoil. No cable machine is assumed. Use instead: Reduce band resistance or diagonal range if the athlete side-bends or loses control. If a suitable secure high anchor is unavailable, stop and re-audit the slot; do not improvise an unsafe anchor. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5 then 1 × 3; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Seated band hip internal rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Cable / Band Chop — standing band execution:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 10 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 4 nonrotational downward slams and 4 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 0 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (0 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 3 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 4 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 8 rounds / 12 side-specific bouts, 68 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 39 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 12 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 70–77 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — held hinge to forefoot finish now carries a held hip start into a forefoot finish, while Landmine Power Row — continuous hip-to-arm drive deliberately links hip impulse to an arm draw rather than preserving the earlier strict row. Independent downward arm release and the constrained-base diagonal slam supply distinct shoulder paths; the class keeps its leg and rotational tasks grounded. Compared with Class 21, prescribed ball releases change from 2 to 14, bilateral foot-landing events change from 2 to 0. Check actual recovery and the larger planned release exposure, then require an organized tall finish without a back arch and a continuous hip-to-arm row sequence. Use the listed light held-hinge rise with flat feet for remaining E1 attempts if release setup fails, recording both lost release and omitted ankle finish; use the paused arm-driven row for remaining E2 work if transfer timing is unqualified. Preserve full retrieval and set rest.

Previous combined Class 21 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 45 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 12 bouts, 68 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_22_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_22.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 23; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 23 of 36

**Stage 8, lesson B: Full-foot rise and alternating traction**

**Primary effort:** Produce a grounded leg rise while transferring arm-force effort between sides.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Band Row — alternating arm draws from a fixed trunk as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Produce a grounded leg rise while transferring arm-force effort between sides.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Band Row — alternating arm draws from a fixed trunk. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Grounded impulse with trunk control:** Band Row — alternating arm draws from a fixed trunk and Kettlebell Swing — continuous two-hand chest-height float: Keep the torso organized while the alternating arms draw or the hips accelerate the retained implement; avoid adding a body rock to the row.
- **E3 + E4 — Supported force into a clear finish:** Kneeling Medicine Ball Chest Pass — tall kneeling, no hip swing and Sit-to-Stand — rapid full-foot push, no flight: Use the declared floor or kneeling support to organize the upward body rise or forward arm release, preserving the arm-only kneeling task.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Forward plant to low rotational chamber and Single-arm rotational underhand medicine-ball scoop: Unilateral low-to-high hand projection and forward plant-to-rotational chamber express rotation without duplicating a full kick.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Band Row — alternating arm draws from a fixed trunk** | **1 × 8** | Transfer backward pulling effort between arms without rocking the torso. Permitted seated foot anchoring; alternate four draws per arm while the other hand remains forward. Each draw returns under control before the next arm begins. Eight total draws, not eight per arm. Use instead: four bilateral seated band-row cycles if alternating control is unsuitable; record the changed laterality and task-rep count. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 3 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E2 | **Kettlebell Swing — continuous two-hand chest-height float** | **1 × 3** | Reapply hip force after the retained implement returns, with no ground landing. Use a light bell and established hinge; three continuous hip-driven swings to chest-level float. Guide the return, keep ribs stacked and park after the third. No arm lift or squat bounce. Use instead: three separated parked swings with full 10 s resets if continuous timing is unconfirmed; add actual time. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E3 | **Kneeling Medicine Ball Chest Pass — tall kneeling, no hip swing** | **1 × 3** | Preserve a small clean forward release while pulling is the main demand. Tall knees, still chest start; push forward with both arms and stay upright. Use instead: a seated chest pass if kneeling is uncomfortable, or a self-anchored band chest press if release clearance fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E4 | **Sit-to-Stand — rapid full-foot push, no flight** | **1 × 3** | Integrate knee and hip extension from an externally supported still start. Sit briefly on an inspected bench that permits full feet on the floor and a comfortable knee angle. Pause 2 s without rocking, stand quickly using the legs, then sit down under control for 3 s. Feet stay flat; do not push with the arms. Use instead: three fast grounded squat rises from a 2 s held comfortable squat if the bench does not fit, with the same reset and no flight. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E5 | **Forward plant to low rotational chamber** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Isolate acceptance-to-free-thigh timing inside the forward-entry kick without another full extension dose. Make one short forward receiving step onto the support foot, turn that foot/pelvis together and lift the free folded knee into a low rotational chamber. Hold 2 s, then set the foot down. Do not extend the lower leg. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: Shorten the step/chamber instead if the athlete rises onto a rigid knee or has to hop. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 8 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Single-arm rotational underhand medicine-ball scoop** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Transmit the turn through a low one-arm swinging release. With a securely grippable light ball beside the rear hip, turn the feet/hips and sweep that hand underhand across the front toward the low target. Keep elbow softly bent, ball in front of the torso and release path clear. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: a two-hand scoop instead if one-hand grip is insecure; do not use a heavier ball. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 8 · E3](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

Use instead: gentle hand resistance through a smaller range at the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension near the finish with the knee angle held, without another lengthened-hinge dose. Secure a manageable load over the pelvis; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Finish with ribs/pelvis aligned, not lumbar arching. Use instead: a lighter loaded floor bridge with the same controlled range; a shorter lever may be used if hamstrings cramp. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P2 | **Band Lat Pulldown — kneeling, two arms** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the downward arm draw needed for assisted pull-up force without another horizontal row set. Use a manufacturer-permitted band on an inspected overhead anchor; kneel tall, draw elbows to ribs, hold 1 s, return 2 s; no lumbar lean. Use instead: a foot-supported pull-up with comfortable grip and enough assistance to preserve 2–3 RIR; otherwise a supported row with the vertical-pull gap recorded. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 2 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P4 | **Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main horizontal pressing force with a reproducible floor-limited range. Lower 2 s, touch upper arms gently, pause 1 s, then press decisively; feet stay down. Coach supervises pickup/set-down. Use instead: a lighter floor press through controlled comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P5 | **Standing Cable or Band Hip Flexion** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Challenge active chamber lifting and controlled lowering so the free leg can accelerate and recover while the other leg supplies the rotational base. Stand upright with a secure band loop at the forefeet, the support foot anchoring its end and optional light fingertip contact on a stable support. Raise the bent knee to a comfortable low chamber with the pelvis quiet, then lower without swinging. Use about 1 second lifting, 2 seconds lowering and 1 second resetting. Do not use momentum, a hop or an unsecured loop. Use instead: Reduce tension or chamber height for pelvic tipping or loss of foot security. If the loop cannot be secured, use self-resisted seated bent-knee hip flexion with the hands resisting the distal thigh instead; record the changed, less reproducible resistance. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 8 · P5](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Cable / Band Lift — standing band execution** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the low-to-high turning path from the hip to the hands and control its return, supporting the selected rising or scooping upper rotational action. Secure a band at a verified low anchor behind one side. Stand in a comfortable split stance. Guide both hands from the rear hip across toward the opposite upper chest, allowing a small foot-and-hip turn. Drive in 1–2 seconds and return in 3 seconds. Keep ribs stacked and stop below any range that requires leaning back or shrugging. No cable machine is assumed. Use instead: Reduce resistance or range if the athlete leans back or loses a controlled return. If a suitable secure low anchor is unavailable, stop and re-audit the slot; do not improvise an unsafe anchor. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 8 · P2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Band Lat Pulldown — kneeling, two arms:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 1 cycles; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Standing Cable or Band Hip Flexion:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Cable / Band Lift — standing band execution:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 3 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 0 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (0 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 6 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 7 rounds / 9 side-specific bouts, 43 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 2 complete walkout cycles (16 heel placements). Preparation: 26 reps/traverses plus 1 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 22 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 58–65 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Band Row — alternating arm draws from a fixed trunk transfers traction between arms while the trunk stays fixed: eight total draws mean four per arm, not eight per arm. The familiar retained swing and small kneeling pass support force production while Forward plant to low rotational chamber introduces forward arrival into a low rotational chamber and Single-arm rotational underhand medicine-ball scoop gives each arm a low-to-high rotational release. Compared with Class 22, prescribed ball releases change from 14 to 7, explosive set/side bouts change from 11 to 8. With actual recovery reviewed, require controlled alternating returns without rocking and a chamber plant that remains balanced. If alternating control fails, use the listed bilateral row conversion only for the remaining equivalent arm work: two unperformed alternating draws become one bilateral cycle, with no restart of the whole set. Use separated parked swings for remaining E2 repetitions if continuous timing is unconfirmed, adding the stated reset time. Stop and log an unmatched single-arm remainder instead of rounding it up to another bilateral cycle.

Previous combined Class 22 prescribed 12 primary bouts and 68 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 9 bouts, 43 reps and 2 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_23_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_23.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 24; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 24 of 36

**Stage 8, lesson C: Ankle advance and forceful draw**

**Primary effort:** Practice separate hip-led low kicking and a descending upper-body rotational release, then regain controlled support. Small ankle advances, grounded leg return and linear arm draws support onset and finish.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Low roundhouse with forward step-down finish as the grounded rotational force anchor. Control residual forward/rotational momentum when the kicking leg becomes the next lead foot.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Low roundhouse with forward step-down finish. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Low roundhouse with forward step-down finish and Two-hand descending backhand medicine-ball throw: Descending upper hand arc and lower kick with a controlled forward recovery finish require distinct endpoint control.
- **E3 + E4 — Compact forward and elbow impulse:** Ankle Pogo — three small forward advances and Band Biceps Curl — loaded return into quick reversal: Keep the action compact as the ankles advance the body or elbows reverse the loaded curl, retaining control before the next effort.
- **E5 + E6 — Grounded return and shoulder draw:** Reverse Lunge to Knee Drive — fast grounded return and Band Straight-Arm Pulldown — rapid shoulder extension: Control the support position through the leg return or shoulder-only pull, finishing without a trunk crunch or unnecessary travel.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Low roundhouse with forward step-down finish** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Control residual forward/rotational momentum when the kicking leg becomes the next lead foot. From a stationary rear-leg stance, execute a low controlled roundhouse and fold the knee, then place that foot into a short forward split stance instead of returning it to the original rear position. Arrest the pelvis with both feet aligned; no spinning through or second kick. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: less kick arc and a shorter step-down instead if the athlete over-rotates or lands across the support foot. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 8 · E6](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Two-hand descending backhand medicine-ball throw** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Deliver outward rotation into a descending diagonal. Start the light ball across the upper chest near the opposite shoulder, not behind the head. Turn and sweep both hands outward/down toward the low forward target, keeping the trunk long. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: Lower the start or shorten the arc if the shoulder is pulled back or the body side-bends. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 8 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Ankle Pogo — three small forward advances** | **1 × 3** | Direct a small ankle-led impulse forward while maintaining a tall aligned body. Three low consecutive bilateral pogos advancing only a little each time; knees remain soft, not locked. Keep the whole series inside the marked short area and absorb/stop the third landing. Use instead: three isolated supported rapid bilateral calf rises without flight or travel, with 10 s between reps and the same 90 s transition, if rhythm, ankle comfort or reception fails; log zero landings and the extra actual reset time. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E4 | **Band Biceps Curl — loaded return into quick reversal** | **1 × 4** | Carry force through the elbow after a controlled lengthening rather than a swinging shoulder start. Band securely beneath both feet, elbows at ribs; lower for 2 s to comfortable near-extension then reverse promptly into flexion. Avoid snapping full elbow extension or leaning. Use instead: a paused rapid neutral-grip curl if reversal control is absent. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E5 | **Reverse Lunge to Knee Drive — fast grounded return** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Drive the stance hip/knee to tall support as the other leg becomes free. Bodyweight: step back under control, then drive through the front whole foot and bring the returning thigh forward to a comfortable knee lift; hold 1 s. Front foot stays grounded. Two per stance leg. Use instead: a quick reverse-lunge return with both feet finishing on the floor at the same per-leg dose if single-support finish fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| E6 | **Band Straight-Arm Pulldown — rapid shoulder extension** | **1 × 3** | Finish with a short shoulder-driven pull that does not add elbow-flexor fatigue. Rated overhead attachment; softly fixed elbows, fast pull from comfortable reach toward thighs, return 2 s with ribs quiet. Use instead: a light controlled rapid dumbbell pullover toward vertical if no suitable attachment exists; record the changed force curve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Band Face Pull — light controlled draw** | **1 × 8** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly strengthen control of the shoulder-blade and upper-arm position during the high-force draw. Band on an inspected chest/face-height attachment; draw toward brow with elbows comfortably out, hold 1 s, return 2 s without leaning. Use instead: a light unanchored band pull-apart if the attachment is unavailable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| S2 | **Seated Band Ankle Eversion — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light outer-ankle strengthening to resist rolling over the foot edge during the push. Heel supported; hand-held light band draws the forefoot inward. Move forefoot outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s while the knee stays still. Use instead: gentle hand resistance through a smaller range at the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Goblet Squat — dumbbell** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Bilateral knee/hip extension through a repeatable full-foot push. Lower 3 s, stand decisively in about 1 s; hold a manageable goblet load and retain comfortable depth without heels lifting. Use instead: a lighter goblet squat through controlled comfortable range; a two-dumbbell front hold may replace a limiting goblet grip. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P2 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Strengthen the wider-elbow backward draw that controls the arm-open position. Hold a still hinge; elbows about 60 degrees from torso, pull to upper ribs, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Use instead: a chest-supported version only on an inspected bench with adequate arm clearance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P3 | **Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force while the knee remains flexed during early push and receiving. Sit on an inspected stable bench, feet on floor; secure loads on thighs, rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without knee-angle change or bouncing. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee calf raise with the same bilateral dose and 2–3 technical RIR if bench/load support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 8 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_08.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 8 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotational Press** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a compound unilateral push for the full-body force work while loading the connection from a freely turning foot and hip to a controlled arm press. Its diagonal pressing path complements the selected rotational task; it does not reproduce every throw path. Secure the landmine and qualify its bar, grip and minimum load. In a modest staggered stance, hold the sleeve near the rear shoulder with the rear hand. Turn the rear foot and hips into a controlled forward-and-up press, keeping wrist stacked and ribs level. No dip-drive, jump or release. Return in 3 seconds and reset without shoulder shrug or lower-back arch. Use instead: Reduce load or range for wrist, elbow or rib-position faults. If the minimum landmine setup is unsuitable, use a one-arm rotational band press from a verified anchor instead; log the changed load and setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 8 · P5](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_08.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Goblet Squat — dumbbell:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.

Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_24_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_24.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).
