## Full Body Force Generation — Class 25 of 36

**Stage 9, lesson A: Step-close vertical and forward release**

**Primary effort:** Time a controlled foot plant with upward leg projection or forward arm release.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Walk-In Vertical Jump — step-close takeoff as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Time a controlled foot plant with upward leg projection or forward arm release.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Walk-In Vertical Jump — step-close takeoff. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Plant before projection:** Walk-In Vertical Jump — step-close takeoff and Medicine Ball Step-In Chest Pass — one forward plant: Organize the controlled foot plant before the upward jump or forward release, retaining the declared step pattern and a balanced finish.
- **E3 + E4 — Controlled reversal with a stable trunk:** Glute Bridge — controlled lower to rapid reversal and Band Lat Pulldown — single-arm rapid elbow drive: Keep the torso supported and organized while the hip reverses upward or the independent arm draws, without adding rotation to either source task.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Retreat-step low roundhouse counter and Lateral-step two-hand medicine-ball backhand throw: Lateral upper entry and retreating lower entry demand controlled direction-specific plants before rotation.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Walk-In Vertical Jump — step-close takeoff** | **2 × 2** | Main conversion of a slow forward entry into an organized upward two-foot push. Take one quiet walking step, bring the second foot alongside, then use a comfortable dip and jump upward; land on two feet and hold 2 s. Exactly two entry foot placements per attempt, no run or preparatory hop. Use instead: a stationary countermovement vertical jump at the same remaining dose if the entry disrupts plant or receiving control; log removed entry steps. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E2 | **Medicine Ball Step-In Chest Pass — one forward plant** | **1 × 3 per lead foot** | Primary timing of a controlled forward plant with a straight-ahead two-arm release. Start behind the marked release line; take one small forward step and release as that foot accepts weight. Rear foot stays grounded; finish balanced without jumping or running through. Alternate lead-foot blocks. Use instead: a stationary shallow-countermovement chest pass if plant/release timing is not demonstrated or the whole step cannot fit the inspected bay; log zero travel steps. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between lead foot bouts; 6 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E3 | **Glute Bridge — controlled lower to rapid reversal** | **1 × 4** | Express hip-finish force after a controlled loaded-position change. Bodyweight, begin in a level bilateral bridge; lower for 2 s until the pelvis lightly meets the floor, then rise rapidly without pausing or arching. Four continuous reps, settling on the floor after the final return. Use instead: four floor-start bridge rises with 10 s full resets and the same 90 s transition if the reversal causes cramp or lumbar compensation; record the extra actual reset time. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E4 | **Band Lat Pulldown — single-arm rapid elbow drive** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Retain a brief independent arm draw after the traveling traction task. Rated overhead attachment, tall stable trunk; quick elbow-to-rib draw and controlled 2 s return, no side bend. Use instead: a supported one-arm dumbbell speed row if overhead anchoring is unavailable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E5 | **Retreat-step low roundhouse counter** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Directly change backward bodyweight travel into a controlled rotational counter action. Make one short retreat step onto the support leg, arrest the backward drift, turn that support foot with the pelvis and execute a low opposite-leg roundhouse. Recoil into a stable stance within the 2 m segment. No opponent or pad. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a smaller/slower retreat or a brief settled plant instead if the athlete continues drifting; do not reach with the kick to compensate. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 9 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Lateral-step two-hand medicine-ball backhand throw** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Connect lateral arrival to an outward upper-body release. Start the ball across the lower ribs; take one short uncrossed lateral step toward the lane, plant, turn and sweep both hands outward into the low target. Hold the finish. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Keep all entry and finish footwork inside a cleared 1-metre start bay; no hop, jump or sprint. Use instead: the stationary backhand throw instead at this dose if arrival causes drift or the shoulder trails behind the trunk. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 9 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Light-Band Pallof Press — fixed split stance** | **1 × 5 per anchor side** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Control unwanted trunk rotation while force passes through a planted lead foot. Rated side attachment at chest height, light tension. Press hands forward 1 s, hold 2 s, return 2 s without rotating; change anchor-facing side after rest. Use instead: a light single-arm supine dumbbell press hold for 1 × 5 repetitions with a 2 s top hold per arm if no side attachment is available; record changed support. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between anchor side bouts; 10 total reps; 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| S2 | **Side-Lying Hip Adduction — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light medial-hip force to maintain a straight stance-leg push without drifting away from the foot. Top foot rests in front of the lower knee. Lift the straight lower leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; pelvis remains stacked. Use instead: a smaller controlled lower-leg lift at the same dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Goblet Squat — dumbbell** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Bilateral knee/hip extension through a repeatable full-foot push. Lower 3 s, stand decisively in about 1 s; hold a manageable goblet load and retain comfortable depth without heels lifting. Use instead: a lighter goblet squat through controlled comfortable range; a two-dumbbell front hold may replace a limiting goblet grip. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P2 | **Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main horizontal pressing force with a reproducible floor-limited range. Lower 2 s, touch upper arms gently, pause 1 s, then press decisively; feet stay down. Coach supervises pickup/set-down. Use instead: a lighter floor press through controlled comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 150 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P3 | **Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force while the knee remains flexed during early push and receiving. Sit on an inspected stable bench, feet on floor; secure loads on thighs, rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without knee-angle change or bouncing. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee calf raise with the same bilateral dose and 2–3 technical RIR if bench/load support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Strengthen the wider-elbow backward draw that controls the arm-open position. Hold a still hinge; elbows about 60 degrees from torso, pull to upper ribs, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Use instead: a chest-supported version only on an inspected bench with adequate arm clearance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 9 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 9 · P1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Goblet Squat — dumbbell:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 6 then 1 × 3. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Loaded Cossack Squat:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 6 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 4 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (8 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 4 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 4 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 8 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 6 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 7 rounds / 8 side-specific bouts, 47 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 32 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 20 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 62–69 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Walk-In Vertical Jump — step-close takeoff introduces one walking step plus a closing foot placement before vertical projection; Medicine Ball Step-In Chest Pass — one forward plant times a single forward plant with a bilateral chest release in both lead-foot conditions. These are controlled entries, not faster run-ups; familiar arm draw and bridge reversal limit additional novelty beside retreating and lateral rotational entries. Compared with Class 24, prescribed ball releases change from 4 to 10, bilateral foot-landing events change from 3 to 4, explosive set/side bouts change from 9 to 11. Review actual recovery and require the entry to organize, rather than disrupt, the takeoff or release line within verified space. Use a stationary countermovement jump for remaining E1 attempts if plant or receiving control fails, and the listed stationary shallow-countermovement chest pass for remaining E2 attempts when step-to-release timing is unqualified. Log removed entry steps, keep release/retrieval rests and add no approach-speed target.

Previous combined Class 24 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 47 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 8 bouts, 47 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_25_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_25.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 26 of 36

**Stage 9, lesson B: Forward plant into controlled projection**

**Primary effort:** Project from a controlled forward entry and regain support before repeating.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Medicine Ball Step-In Shot Put — forward-facing single arm as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Project from a controlled forward entry and regain support before repeating.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Medicine Ball Step-In Shot Put — forward-facing single arm. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Forward entry into a directed impulse:** Medicine Ball Step-In Shot Put — forward-facing single arm and Walk-In Broad Jump — step-close forward takeoff: Time the controlled forward entry to the leg projection or independent arm release, then regain support without running through.
- **E3 + E4 — Push from stable support and finish controlled:** Standing Wall Power Push-Off — return to upright and Step-Down to Step-Up — quick grounded reversal: Use a secure support contact to produce the next push, finishing upright or on the step; neither task adds a drop or a rebound hand catch.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Pre-chambered hip-unwind step-down and Drop-step rotational medicine-ball shot-put: Upper drop-step pushing release and lower supported hip unwind express force out of a loaded orientation.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Medicine Ball Step-In Shot Put — forward-facing single arm** | **1 × 2 per arm** | Connect the opposite-foot plant to an independent arm release without transverse rotation. Small step with the foot opposite the throwing arm; push a manageable ball forward as that foot plants. No side-on preload or follow-through step. Use instead: the fixed-stance forward-facing shot-put throw if step timing or ball control fails; use a self-anchored band press if containment is absent. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between arm bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E2 | **Walk-In Broad Jump — step-close forward takeoff** | **1 × 2** | Direct the same modest entry into a short forward leg projection. Take one quiet walking step and close the other foot, then make one modest two-foot forward jump; hold the two-foot landing 2 s. No running, added hop or distance target. Use instead: a stationary broad jump at the same remaining dose if the entry or total travel cannot fit; log removed entry steps. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E3 | **Standing Wall Power Push-Off — return to upright** | **1 × 3** | Express a brief horizontal push while the body moves away from a fixed hand contact. At an inspected structural wall, feet fixed and close enough for a modest lean. Hands at chest height; push quickly into brief hand separation and return only to balanced upright. Slowly reset the lean; no rebound hand catch or backward step. Use instead: a non-flight fast incline push-up if wall structure or balance is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E4 | **Step-Down to Step-Up — quick grounded reversal** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Accept a small controlled lowering then push through the lead foot without flight. Stand with both feet on a low inspected step. Keep the lead foot planted, lower the other heel to a light floor touch in 2 s, then drive back up quickly until both feet join. Two complete down-and-up reps per lead leg. Do not bounce from the floor foot. Use instead: a shallow supported single-leg squat lower-and-rise at the same per-leg dose if the step height or path is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E5 | **Pre-chambered hip-unwind step-down** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Recover the rotational base when the free leg begins suspended instead of available on the floor. Start with a low folded-knee chamber and the pelvis comfortably turned, using light fingertip support if needed. Briskly unwind the pelvis with a freely turning support foot while bringing the raised leg back to a rear stance position; finish still. No kick extension. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: a smaller starting turn/chamber instead for foot sticking or a slapping return; do not torque the standing knee backward. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 9 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Drop-step rotational medicine-ball shot-put** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Use a rear-hip reload to drive a directed pressing release. Ball at rear shoulder. Open the lead foot slightly away/back, settle pressure into the rear hip, then pivot and push toward the low target with the rear hand. Do not cross feet or lean away. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Keep all entry and finish footwork inside a cleared 1-metre start bay; no hop, jump or sprint. Use instead: the standing shot-put instead if the reload disrupts wrist or base control. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 9 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Bilateral Band External Rotation — elbows at sides** | **1 × 8** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light shoulder-rotator strengthening to control upper-arm position during the day's press and pull. Unanchored band between hands, elbows at ribs; rotate outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s without shrugging. Use instead: a lower-tension band and shorter comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |

Back against an inspected wall, heels on floor; lift forefeet 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Adjust foot distance to stay easy. Use instead: a seated unweighted toe raise through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P2 | **One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench** | **2 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the arm-driven backward draw while support prevents torso rotation. Draw elbow toward hip, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; stop before the shoulder rolls forward or the trunk turns. Use instead: a thigh-supported dumbbell row with the same arm path if a bench does not fit. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 60 s between arm bouts; 24 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 2 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow extension for the finish of a forceful press. Neutral grips, upper arms near vertical; lower 2 s beside the ears, extend under control without shoulder swing. Use instead: a lighter floor-supported elbow extension through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 9 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotational Press** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a compound unilateral push for the full-body force work while loading the connection from a freely turning foot and hip to a controlled arm press. Its diagonal pressing path complements the selected rotational task; it does not reproduce every throw path. Secure the landmine and qualify its bar, grip and minimum load. In a modest staggered stance, hold the sleeve near the rear shoulder with the rear hand. Turn the rear foot and hips into a controlled forward-and-up press, keeping wrist stacked and ribs level. No dip-drive, jump or release. Return in 3 seconds and reset without shoulder shrug or lower-back arch. Use instead: Reduce load or range for wrist, elbow or rib-position faults. If the minimum landmine setup is unsuitable, use a one-arm rotational band press from a verified anchor instead; log the changed load and setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 9 · P2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 1 cycles; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Seated band hip internal rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotational Press:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 4 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 2 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (4 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 4 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 4 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 4 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 7 rounds / 11 side-specific bouts, 57 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 2 complete walkout cycles (16 heel placements). Preparation: 32 reps/traverses plus 1 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 13 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 62–69 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Medicine Ball Step-In Shot Put — forward-facing single arm changes the controlled plant-to-release task to the arm opposite the stepping foot, while Walk-In Broad Jump — step-close forward takeoff directs the step-close leg impulse forward. The wall push ends upright without a loaded hand reception, and the step reversal remains grounded; the lower rotational unwind and upper drop-step press add separate controlled turn constraints. Compared with Class 25, prescribed ball releases change from 10 to 8, bilateral foot-landing events change from 4 to 2, explosive set/side bouts change from 11 to 10. With actual recovery and the complete travel envelope checked, require a balanced opposite-foot release and controlled forward landing. Use the fixed-stance forward-facing shot put for remaining E1 attempts if step timing fails, and a stationary broad jump for remaining E2 attempts if entry or total travel cannot fit. Record removed steps without inventing removed hand landings, since the wall task prescribes none.

Previous combined Class 25 prescribed 8 primary bouts and 47 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 11 bouts, 57 reps and 2 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_26_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_26.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 27; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 27 of 36

**Stage 9, lesson C: Backward traction and forward leg drive**

**Primary effort:** Generate compact folded-leg and hand sweeps through controlled hip and trunk turns. Grounded lunge return, leg-driven forward projection and linear arm traction supply plant and finish support.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Low bent-knee hook shadow sweep as the grounded rotational force anchor. Practice the coupled inward thigh sweep and knee folding used to recover a rotational leg action.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Low bent-knee hook shadow sweep. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Low bent-knee hook shadow sweep and Short crossover-to-band backhand speed sweep: Upper crossover-to-retained sweep and low folding hook sweep retain transverse outputs without another airborne task.
- **E3 + E4 — Plant to direct force:** Forward Lunge — rapid return to the starting foot line and Battle-Rope Traction Pull — one backward step per draw: Use the declared forward or backward plant to organize the connected leg or arm effort, then stop and reset within the controlled movement corridor.
- **E5 + E6 — Leg impulse into implement force:** Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — walk-step forward release and Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted: Sequence the declared leg drive into the guided press or scoop release, keeping the finish balanced and free of an extra jump.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Low bent-knee hook shadow sweep** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Practice the coupled inward thigh sweep and knee folding used to recover a rotational leg action. From a comfortable open kick stance, lift the free thigh low, extend only slightly and sweep inward while folding the knee, then place the foot under control. Keep the arc small, below hip height, and coordinate the support-foot turn; no spin or contact. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: an entirely folded-knee inward hook instead if the longer lever cannot be controlled; stop for groin or knee discomfort. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 9 · E5](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E2 | **Short crossover-to-band backhand speed sweep** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Brake the entry while accelerating and stopping a retained transverse load. With a light side-anchored band, take a small step behind and replant the lead foot. After a secure plant, turn and sweep both hands across the chest-height arc, then return slowly and reset feet. Verify the stated band anchor and tension; retain the band throughout, with no release or uncontrolled recoil. Keep all entry and finish footwork inside a cleared 1-metre start bay; no hop, jump or sprint. Use instead: the stationary sweep instead if feet or band line cross unsafely. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 9 · E4](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_09.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E3 | **Forward Lunge — rapid return to the starting foot line** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Accept a controlled forward step and drive the front leg to regain the start. Bodyweight: step forward gently, lower under control, then push through the front whole foot to return quickly beside the other foot. Stay grounded, trunk quiet and knee aligned. Two complete returns per moving leg. Use instead: a supported stationary split-squat rapid rise at the same per-leg dose if the forward entry cannot stay controlled. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E4 | **Battle-Rope Traction Pull — one backward step per draw** | **1 × 4** | Transfer a controlled backward plant into a hand-led traction effort. With a manageable free rope laid in the inspected corridor, take one small backward step while drawing the rope toward the hip, then stop and reset. Alternate two draws per arm; no rope around the body or fixed load attached. Use instead: the seated hand-over-hand free-rope pull for four total draws if traction/step control or clearance is unsuitable; log zero steps. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 8 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E5 | **Medicine Ball Scoop Toss — walk-step forward release** | **1 × 1 per leg** | Transfer a controlled forward step through the planted leg into a straight release. Start standing with a light ball and long arms. Take one short forward walking step, let the rear foot remain grounded in split stance, then drive legs/hips to release forward. One throw per lead leg; no airborne takeoff, torso twist or catch. Use instead: one rapid supported split-stance hip-hinge return per lead leg with no ball, using the same side recovery, if release containment fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between leg bouts; 2 total reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| E6 | **Two-Hand Landmine Push Press — shallow dip, feet stay planted** | **1 × 3** | Reinforce leg-to-arm timing against a guided load with no travel. Short dip, extend into the press without jumping; grip stays secure, lower 2 s and reset fully. Use instead: a strict light landmine press if timing is unsuitable or a self-anchored band press if bar mass is excessive. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 8 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · E5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

Press hands forward 1 s, hold 2 s, return 2 s without rotating; change anchor-facing side after rest. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |

| P1 | **Reverse Lunge — two dumbbells** | **1 × 4 per leg** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Accept a controlled step and drive through the front leg to regain the start. Step back quietly, lower under control and push through the whole front foot to stand; about 4 s per complete cycle. Do not launch off the trailing toes. Use instead: a stationary split squat at the same per-leg dose if stepping reduces control. Use instead: a hands-elevated push-up on an inspected immovable support. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |

Plantar-flexion force with the knees extended for the final upward/forward push. Use stable hand support if needed; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to the floor. Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 9 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_09.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. **Delivery and timing:** Estimate 59–66 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Low bent-knee hook shadow sweep uses a compact folded-leg hook arc, paired with the retained crossover-to-hand sweep of Short crossover-to-band backhand speed sweep. The grounded lunge return and Battle-Rope Traction Pull — one backward step per draw connect a deliberate plant to forward leg force or backward traction; the small walk-step scoop and familiar guided press support force transfer without new foot reception. Compared with Class 26, prescribed ball releases change from 8 to 2, bilateral foot-landing events change from 2 to 0. Confirm actual recovery and require the folded-leg sweep to stop under control, with feet clear of each other and the band line before the upper turn. Use the entirely folded-knee hook for remaining E1 attempts if the longer lever is unqualified, and the stationary band sweep for remaining E2 attempts when the crossover cannot be organized. Preserve reset and side rests; no extra kick, draw or crossover is added.

Previous combined Class 26 prescribed 11 primary bouts and 57 conventional/traverse reps plus 2 cycles; this class prescribes 8 bouts, 46 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. This third lesson completes stage coverage and repeats the selected light supports for the stated shoulder/hip/foot jobs. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities.
