## Full Body Force Generation — Class 28 of 36

**Stage 10, lesson A: Mid-range force and complete leg finish**

**Primary effort:** Drive through a defined finish range while producing a clear upward leg impulse.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Countermovement Jump — vertical rise and stick as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Drive through a defined finish range while producing a clear upward leg impulse.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Countermovement Jump — vertical rise and stick. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Force from a defined start:** Countermovement Jump — vertical rise and stick and Band Chest Press — mid-range pause into fast finish: Respect the declared start boundary before accelerating toward the jump or press finish; do not add a second dip after the arm's mid-range pause.
- **E3 + E4 — Support pressure through the finish:** Split-Stance Calf Rise — rapid rear-heel finish and Band Row — mid-range pause into fast finish: Keep pressure organized through the support stance while the rear heel rises or arms complete the held-start draw, without a torso swing.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Point-cued open-step hip turn and Single-arm rotational medicine-ball hook throw: Unilateral upper hook release and early point-cued lower turn pair a hand-force task with a modest decision demand; no midmovement cues.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Countermovement Jump — vertical rise and stick** | **2 × 3** | Main coordinated knee/hip push after one controlled countermovement. Use one comfortable dip with arm swing, jump vertically, receive on both feet and hold 2 s. No repeated bouncing, deep-collapse dip or knee tuck for apparent height. Use instead: a smaller countermovement jump or fast nonflight squat rise when reception cannot stay controlled; record removed landings. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E2 | **Band Chest Press — mid-range pause into fast finish** | **2 × 3** | Primary force expression through the latter pressing range without losing rib or wrist position. Self-anchored permitted band across upper back; begin with elbows partly extended, hold 2 s, then finish the press quickly without snapping lockout. Return fully under control before setting the next start. Use instead: a full-range light band speed press if the partial start cannot be held comfortably. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E3 | **Split-Stance Calf Rise — rapid rear-heel finish** | **1 × 3 per leg** | Produce rear-ankle force through the forefoot in a stable forward-oriented stance. Use stable hand support and a short split stance. Front foot stays flat; rear knee softly straight and rear forefoot planted. Rapidly lift the rear heel, hold 1 s and lower 2 s while the pelvis stays still. Three reps per rear leg; no stride or flight. Use instead: an assisted single-leg straight-knee rapid heel rise at the same per-leg dose if the split stance does not load the rear ankle usefully. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 6 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E4 | **Band Row — mid-range pause into fast finish** | **1 × 4** | Express pulling force in the finish without substituting trunk extension. Permitted seated foot anchoring, begin elbows lightly bent; hold still 2 s, then draw toward lower ribs quickly. Return 2 s and reset; shoulders do not roll forward. Use instead: a full-range paused light band row if the partial start causes strain. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E5 | **Point-cued open-step hip turn** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Select the support/turn direction before the leg-driven release. Begin square and still. After the coach points left or right, take one short opening step and turn the feet/pelvis toward that line, then hold a kick stance 2 s. Cue is given before any movement; no kick or jump. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: an announced direction instead if cue errors dominate; preserve the same physical drill and record a planned version. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 10 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Single-arm rotational medicine-ball hook throw** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Couple a bent-arm transverse release to the trunk turn. Hold a securely grippable light ball at the rear side of the chest, elbow bent near 90 degrees. Pivot and turn while sweeping the bent forearm through a short forward/transverse arc into the low target. Avoid a long backswing or forceful elbow lock. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: the two-hand backhand throw instead if one-hand grip or shoulder position is insecure. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 10 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Scapular Push-Up — quadruped, dynamic** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly load controlled shoulder-blade travel for pushing support and force transfer. Hands and knees down; keep elbows straight. Settle chest 2 s, push away 2 s, hold 2 s without trunk sag. Use instead: a smaller controlled scapular range with more weight supported at knees. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| S2 | **Seated Band Ankle Eversion — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light outer-ankle strengthening to resist rolling over the foot edge during the push. Heel supported; hand-held light band draws the forefoot inward. Move forefoot outward 2 s, hold 1 s, return 2 s while the knee stays still. Use instead: gentle hand resistance through a smaller range at the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · S1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Goblet Squat — dumbbell** | **2 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Bilateral knee/hip extension through a repeatable full-foot push. Lower 3 s, stand decisively in about 1 s; hold a manageable goblet load and retain comfortable depth without heels lifting. Use instead: a lighter goblet squat through controlled comfortable range; a two-dumbbell front hold may replace a limiting goblet grip. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P2 | **Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip** | **2 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Main horizontal pressing force with a reproducible floor-limited range. Lower 2 s, touch upper arms gently, pause 1 s, then press decisively; feet stay down. Coach supervises pickup/set-down. Use instead: a lighter floor press through controlled comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 150 s between complete set rounds; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P3 | **Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force with the knees extended for the final upward/forward push. Use stable hand support if needed; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s to the floor. Keep knees softly straight and heels tracking over the forefoot. Use instead: a lighter supported standing calf raise; use individual load or leverage that retains the required technical reserve. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Strengthen the wider-elbow backward draw that controls the arm-open position. Hold a still hinge; elbows about 60 degrees from torso, pull to upper ribs, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Use instead: a chest-supported version only on an inspected bench with adequate arm clearance. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 10 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. ## Full Body Force Generation — Class 29 of 36

**Stage 10, lesson B: Supported ascent and shoulder force**

**Primary effort:** Express a supported lower-body rise and a brief shoulder-driven upper action.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Dumbbell Reverse Fly — quick lift, controlled return as the controlled upper-body force anchor. Express a supported lower-body rise and a brief shoulder-driven upper action.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Dumbbell Reverse Fly — quick lift, controlled return. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Supported ascent and shoulder opening:** Dumbbell Reverse Fly — quick lift, controlled return and Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins: Stabilize the body through the support leg or held hinge so the intended knee drive or shoulder opening supplies the motion.
- **E3 + E4 — Controlled hip and hand push:** Standing Wall Power Push-Off — return to upright and Hip Thrust — bench-supported rapid rise: Use stable support to produce the hip rise or hand push-off, finishing tall without lumbar extension or an added rebound.
- **E5 + E6 — Supported rotational force:** Coach-called-leg low roundhouse and Med Ball Rotational Scoop Toss — paused floor-target start: Familiar settled upper scoop lowers novelty while a called lower leg changes preparation responsibility.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Dumbbell Reverse Fly — quick lift, controlled return** | **1 × 4** | Express shoulder-driven backward force without elbow flexion carrying the movement. Very light dumbbells, stable hip hinge, elbows softly fixed; open arms only to a comfortable line with the torso, then lower 2 s. No shrug, trunk lift or ballistic weight release. Use instead: an unloaded same-path reverse fly with quick controlled opening if even light dumbbells disrupt alignment. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E2 | **Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins** | **1 × 2 per leg** | Let one lead leg supply upward force against a stable support without hand-off to a jump. Use a low inspected immovable step. Front whole foot planted; rise quickly without springing from the trailing foot, bring that foot onto the step, then descend under control in 3 s. No flight. Use instead: a supported fast split-squat rise with both feet grounded and the same per-leg dose if support fit fails. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s between leg bouts; 4 total reps; 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E3 | **Standing Wall Power Push-Off — return to upright** | **1 × 3** | Keep a short fixed-contact pushing task with a clear balanced finish and no hand reception. Inspected wall, modest lean, feet fixed; push into brief hand separation and only to balanced upright, then reset slowly. Use instead: a non-flight fast incline push-up if wall structure or balance is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E4](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E4 | **Hip Thrust — bench-supported rapid rise** | **1 × 3** | Express hip extension through the finish with shoulder support allowing a different range from the floor bridge. Use bodyweight first. Support the upper back, not the neck, on a low stable bench that cannot slide; feet flat and knees comfortable. Lower 2 s, drive hips up quickly and hold 1 s with ribs and pelvis aligned. No lumbar arch or head throw. Use instead: three rapid floor-bridge rises with the same reset/rest if bench height, friction or shoulder support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| E5 | **Coach-called-leg low roundhouse** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Directly link support-leg selection to the hip-led kick without an extra entry task. Start from a still parallel athletic stance. The coach names the kicking leg before movement. Shift to the other leg, turn the support foot with the pelvis and perform a low roundhouse with active recoil and a controlled return. Complete the written side dose using the stated side-bout rest; vary the early call only within that schedule and never add calls or attempts. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a preannounced leg instead if the choice causes a rushed plant; cue accuracy never overrides safe mechanics. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 10 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Med Ball Rotational Scoop Toss — paused floor-target start** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Retain a settled-start whole-chain release reference. Stand side-on with both hands holding the ball near the rear hip for 2 seconds. Pivot, turn and sweep toward the low target; no entry or countermovement. Hold the finish for 2 seconds. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Use instead: Reduce ball mass/turn range if arms lead or balance is lost. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 10 · E3](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Dumbbell Wrist Extension — forearm supported** | **1 × 6 per arm** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly reinforce wrist alignment at the finish of loaded presses and pulls. Forearm supported, palm down; very light load, lift 1 s and lower 3 s without gripping maximally. Use instead: unloaded wrist extension if a light implement still causes compensation. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between arm bouts; 12 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · S2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |

Light lateral-hip strengthening to keep the pelvis and knee organized over the force-producing leg. Lie on side, lower leg bent; lift upper leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s with toes facing forward and pelvis still. Bodyweight is sufficient unless clearly easy. Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension near the finish with the knee angle held, without another lengthened-hinge dose. Secure a manageable load over the pelvis; rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s. Finish with ribs/pelvis aligned, not lumbar arching. Use instead: a lighter loaded floor bridge with the same controlled range; a shorter lever may be used if hamstrings cramp. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P3](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P2 | **One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench** | **2 × 6 per arm** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the arm-driven backward draw while support prevents torso rotation. Draw elbow toward hip, hold 1 s, lower 2 s; stop before the shoulder rolls forward or the trunk turns. Use instead: a thigh-supported dumbbell row with the same arm path if a bench does not fit. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s between complete set rounds; 60 s between arm bouts; 24 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P2](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P3 | **Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees** | **1 × 6** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Plantar-flexion force while the knee remains flexed during early push and receiving. Sit on an inspected stable bench, feet on floor; secure loads on thighs, rise 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s without knee-angle change or bouncing. Use instead: a supported standing bent-knee calf raise with the same bilateral dose and 2–3 technical RIR if bench/load support is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P5](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow extension for the finish of a forceful press. Neutral grips, upper arms near vertical; lower 2 s beside the ears, extend under control without shoulder swing. Use instead: a lighter floor-supported elbow extension through comfortable range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P5](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P5 | **Loaded Cossack Squat** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Supply a substantial lateral knee-and-hip compound, strengthening weight acceptance and return over the stance leg that supports the selected lower rotational action. Hold a suitable dumbbell at the chest in a comfortable wide stance. Sit toward the working hip as the other leg lengthens, keeping the loaded foot supported and the knee following the toes. Return to centre under control. Use about 2 seconds lowering, 1 second lifting and 1 second organising the next rep. Depth is individually qualified; do not force a deep groin stretch or turn on a fixed knee. Use instead: a shallower controlled Cossack range and adjust the dumbbell load for knee or pelvic-position faults. Stop for groin pain or inability to qualify a challenging controlled range; do not add a second movement. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 10 · P3](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 10 · P2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Dumbbell Glute Bridge — floor-supported:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — One-Arm Row — dumbbell, hand and knee on bench:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5 per arm. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, 30 seconds between arms, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Seated Dumbbell Calf Raise — bent knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Skull Crusher — floor-supported:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Loaded Cossack Squat:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 0 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 0 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 0 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (0 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 7 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 4 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 7 rounds / 10 side-specific bouts, 59 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 33 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 22 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 61–68 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Dumbbell Reverse Fly — quick lift, controlled return gives the shoulder-opening action a small rapid task without elbow flexion supplying the draw; Step-Up — fast grounded drive, rear foot joins retains grounded lead-leg ascent. The supported hip-thrust finish changes the hip range from floor bridges, while the familiar paused rotational scoop limits novelty beside an early called-leg kick. Compared with Class 28, prescribed bilateral foot-landing events change from 6 to 0, explosive set/side bouts change from 11 to 9, kick actions change from 0 to 4. Require actual recovery, a stable hinge during the fly and an upper-back support that does not slide or load the neck during the hip thrust. Use an unloaded same-path opening for remaining E1 attempts if the dumbbell disrupts alignment, and the listed floor bridge for remaining E4 attempts if bench fit fails. Preannounce the kicking leg for remaining E5 attempts if the call produces a rushed plant; keep the stated side schedule.

Previous combined Class 28 prescribed 9 primary bouts and 52 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 10 bouts, 59 reps and 0 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_29_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_29.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 30 of 36

**Stage 10, lesson C: Split-base push and catch-set release**

**Primary effort:** Initiate a compact hip-turning thigh action from the early cue and express a separate unloaded upper-body backhand arc. Split-base leg force and catch-set forward release support an organized start and finish.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Auditory-go pre-chambered thigh whip as the grounded rotational force anchor. Release proximal rotation from a prepared leg position without anticipating the start.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Auditory-go pre-chambered thigh whip. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Turn and control the finish:** Auditory-go pre-chambered thigh whip and Unloaded two-hand rotational backhand sweep: Unloaded upper backhand and early auditory-go folded-thigh sweep produce compact rotational arcs with no release or jump.
- **E3 + E4 — Receive control before another impulse:** Split Squat Jump — fixed stance, same-stance reception and Medicine Ball Catch-Set Chest Pass — gentle coach feed: Control the prescribed foot reception or gentle ball feed before repeating, preserving the split-base landing and the set-before-release requirement.
- **E5 + E6 — Retained-load acceleration with a steady finish:** Kettlebell Swing — continuous two-hand chest-height float and Band Biceps Curl — rapid neutral-grip pull: Keep the body organized as the hips or elbows accelerate their retained load, then control the return without an extra trunk swing.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Auditory-go pre-chambered thigh whip** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Release proximal rotation from a prepared leg position without anticipating the start. Set a low bent-knee chamber on the named leg. Wait still for the coach go word, then make one short brisk roundhouse thigh sweep with the knee folded and support foot free to turn; reset fully. Vary the go timing modestly, without long fatiguing holds. Let the support foot or both feet turn freely with the pelvis; never force rotation through a trapped knee. Remain grounded. Use instead: a predictable countdown instead if waiting posture fails; keep the pre-cue hold at or below about 3 s. Palms close together across the lower ribs, elbows soft. Pivot and sweep both hands outward in front of the body, stop within the comfortable arc, then reset slowly. Use instead: Shorten the arc if shoulders trail behind the torso. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. From a comfortable fixed split stance make one small dip and jump a modest height. Receive with both feet in the same split stance and hold 2 s; no scissor switch or rear-foot kick. Two attempts per lead leg. Use instead: two fast grounded supported split-squat rises per lead leg if the split reception cannot be controlled; log removed landings. Band secured under feet, elbows at ribs; quick flexion, lower 2 s, no hip swing or shoulder lift. Use instead: a light simultaneous dumbbell hammer curl with rapid controlled ascent if band fit is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · E6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |

### 3. Strength (Stabilization/Resilience) — Exactly 2 Exercises

Exactly two light strengthening tasks, one for each region. Keep at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; do not chase burn or fatigue. Preserve the source tempo and any counted hold/relaxation. Stop or reduce range/load if control fades.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Scapular Push-Up — quadruped, dynamic** | **1 × 6** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Lightly load controlled shoulder-blade travel for pushing support and force transfer. Hands and knees down; keep elbows straight. Settle chest 2 s, push away 2 s, hold 2 s without trunk sag. Use instead: a smaller controlled scapular range with more weight supported at knees. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 45 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · S1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| S2 | **Side-Lying Hip Abduction — light** | **1 × 5 per leg** | Keep this light support at ≥5 technical RIR. Light lateral-hip strengthening to keep the pelvis and knee organized over the force-producing leg. Lie on side, lower leg bent; lift upper leg slightly 2 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s with toes facing forward and pelvis still. Bodyweight is sufficient unless clearly easy. Use instead: a smaller unloaded leg lift with the same per-leg dose. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 30 s between leg bouts; 10 total reps; 60 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · S2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |

### 4. Strength (Primary) — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six challenging primary-strength tasks: two from each force course plus one upper and one lower rotational support. Use about 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve with controlled lowering/range. No forced reps, failure/max testing or breathless circuit. Same-lift preparation is separate from working sets; extra load finding adds actual work/time.

The original force-course doses and preparation stay with their source exercises. Added rotational support uses the clearly stated smaller combined dose and its own easy preparation. A new class or changed position in the sequence does not justify a load increase. Judge late-session technical reserve and record reductions rather than forcing nominal completion.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells** | **1 × 5** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Hip extension from a lengthened hinge, with the trunk transmitting rather than supplying the motion. Lower 3 s with soft knees, bells close and hips back; stand in about 1 s. Stop range before back rounding or pelvic tuck. Use instead: a lighter two-dumbbell hinge through a shorter controlled range. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P2](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P2 | **Push-Up — controlled full repetition** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the press with free shoulder-blade movement and a steady trunk. Lower 2 s, push decisively, finish tall through shoulders for 1 s; no hand flight. Adjust leverage or band resistance to reach 2–3 RIR. Use instead: a hands-elevated push-up on an inspected immovable support. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 120 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P3 | **Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle** | **1 × 2 cycles** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Knee-flexor leverage while the pelvis stays raised, distinct from moving through a hip hinge. Start in a bilateral bridge. Each cycle is two small heel steps out per foot, then two back per foot: eight placements in about 8 s. Keep a soft knee and level hips; never chase a fully straight leg. Use instead: a shorter-lever walkout at the same complete-cycle count; reduce range for sagging or cramp and stop if control does not return. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P6](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P4 | **Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms** | **1 × 8** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load elbow flexors for finishing the backward draw without another shoulder-extension set. Upper arms stay at ribs; lift 1 s, hold 1 s, lower 2 s, without hip swing or wrist collapse. Use instead: a lighter neutral-grip curl without body movement. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 10 · P6](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_10.md). |
| P5 | **Seated band hip internal rotation** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load controlled internal-rotation torque directly at the hip, complementing the larger leg push used to organise the selected grounded lower turn. Sit on a stable bench with thighs supported and hips and knees near 90 degrees. Fit a secure loop around the lower shins. Keep the other foot planted while the working foot moves a short distance outward, lightly clear of the floor, through hip internal rotation. Keep that knee and the pelvis still. Take 2 seconds outward and 2 seconds returning; do not force end range or twist the knee or ankle. Use instead: Reduce range or band tension for knee, ankle or pelvic movement. If the loop cannot be fitted securely, use self-resisted seated hip internal rotation with the opposite hand resisting the outside of the lower shin instead; manual resistance is a changed and less reproducible load. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side bouts; 10 total reps; 90 s before the next lift preparation. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 10 · P4](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |
| P6 | **Landmine Rotation** | **1 × 6 alternating traverses total** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the turning arc and controlled endpoints that transmit lower-body force into the selected upper rotational action. Secure the landmine and qualify the bar, grip and minimum load. Hold the sleeve or suitable grip in both hands with elbows softly bent. Turn the feet and hips with a modest side-to-side loaded arc while keeping ribs over pelvis. Each one-way traverse is one rep; the return is the next counted rep. Take about 2 seconds per traverse and settle briefly at each endpoint. Avoid trunk lean, a trapped knee or an uncontrolled finish. Use instead: Reduce the load or arc for loss of alignment. If a secured, manageable landmine setup cannot be qualified, use a standing two-hand band rotation with a verified side anchor and the same one-way traverse counting instead; log the changed setup. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** Finish this working bout; no additional set or finisher is prescribed. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 10 · P2](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_10.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Romanian Deadlift — two dumbbells:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Push-Up — controlled full repetition:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 3. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Hamstring Walkout — complete out-and-back cycle:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 1 cycles; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Hammer Curl — simultaneous arms:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Seated band hip internal rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Landmine Rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4 alternating one-way traverses total, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 3 nonrotational and 0 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 3 source ball-receiving events and 0 rotational handoffs counted separately. 4 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (8 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 3 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 0 kick actions) and 4 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 0 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 6 rounds / 7 side-specific bouts, 37 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 2 complete walkout cycles (16 heel placements). Preparation: 23 reps/traverses plus 1 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 16 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.

**Delivery and timing:** Estimate 52–59 minutes after Access & Prepare 1 with prompt equipment access. Includes selected work, resets, side/set recovery, new-order transitions, same-lift preparation and 12–18 minutes extra instruction/setup. Additional recovery, queues or load finding add actual time. Existing preparation and complete booking duration remain unresolved. Never shorten needed recovery to fit a clock. Class numbers specify 36 ordered exposures, not consecutive days or a fixed 36-week calendar. Review actual sport, lifting, throwing, kicking, running, jumping and separate tumbling before scheduling. Roughly 48–72 hours between comparably demanding exposures is an initial planning allowance, adjusted to actual recovery. Do not also perform the standalone source workouts or double up missed classes. No athlete completion or readiness is inferred from a saved class.

**Setup:** Inspect all loads, stable supports, band/landmine attachments, surfaces and athlete fit. Use the three separate 10 m lanes with complete setup, action and receiving/stopping envelopes inside the available space; do not combine them into a 30 m runway. Clear waiting/return paths outside active ball and swept-leg areas. Ball flight, impact, bounce and roll containment require a separately verified area; no throwing wall or rebound behavior is assumed. Only tasks that explicitly prescribe a feed or receiving action include it; all other releases are no-catch. Retrieve only after the ball stops and the coach clears entry into the area. Low kicks are noncontact and below hip height, with active recoil and no knee snap or spin; qualify kicking competence during the prescribed first attempts. For a handoff, secure the ball and let the feeder release and step clear before turning; no airborne feed is added. Finish each exercise and its recovery, then clear/reset the area before the next. Mechanical connections do not authorize simultaneous jump/kick/throw combinations or supersets. Before multiple substitutions, check that they do not duplicate another slot; otherwise stop the affected work and record a modified/incomplete delivery for revision.

**Progression note:** Auditory-go pre-chambered thigh whip begins from a low prepared chamber and responds to an early go cue; Unloaded two-hand rotational backhand sweep keeps the upper rotational arc unloaded. Split Squat Jump — fixed stance, same-stance reception adds a modest same-split-stance foot reception, while Medicine Ball Catch-Set Chest Pass — gentle coach feed revisits catch-set forward release with a deliberate pause rather than immediate return. Compared with Class 29, prescribed ball releases change from 4 to 3, bilateral foot-landing events change from 0 to 4, airborne feed catches change from 0 to 3. Review actual recovery and require the brief chamber wait without postural loss, a free support-foot turn and a split landing that can hold. Use the listed predictable countdown for remaining E1 attempts if waiting fails, keeping its pre-cue hold at or below about 3 seconds. Replace remaining split jumps with grounded supported rises when receiving is unqualified; use self-started chest passes for remaining E4 attempts if catching is unsuitable and record removed catches.

Previous combined Class 29 prescribed 10 primary bouts and 59 conventional/traverse reps plus 0 cycles; this class prescribes 7 bouts, 37 reps and 2 cycles. This is a redistributed task dose, not an earned load increase. This third lesson completes stage coverage and repeats the selected light supports for the stated shoulder/hip/foot jobs. Original force doses/within-task recovery and same-lift preparation are retained; rotational additions are separately reduced. Advance only after actual controlled execution at the target reserve and satisfactory recovery. Hold, reduce or revise if feedback or first-attempt quality does not support the new constraint.

**Audit summary:** All 14 slots have a specific job, nonzero dose, cue, recovery and replacement. The six explosive slots include all four requested sources; the first task and three connections define the day's effort. Each retained source-force entry keeps its original set/rep/side dose, and added rotation uses a separate reduced dose with original provenance. No rotational landing is added. Similar muscle groups receive complementary initiation, transfer and finishing jobs; the light supports stay easy. The extra light supports are disclosed repeats rather than new work identities. Working/preparation time was recalculated after reordering; old source phase transitions are replaced by today's explicit recovery. Full duration and athlete response remain unknown. See [class audit](audits/class_30_audit.md), [workload](workload_class_30.json) and [stage coverage](source_coverage.json).

**Handoff:** Review actual delivery and this saved workload before Class 31; develop its complementary job without assuming these prescriptions were performed.

## Full Body Force Generation — Class 31 of 36

**Stage 11, lesson A: Receive and project from controlled starts**

**Primary effort:** Link a controlled contact or plant to the next clear force action.

**Baseline:** Ages 12–14 with established lifting/landing technique, individually qualified ballistic/kicking skill and supervision. All athlete execution, load and recovery remain unreported.

**Daily stimulus brief:** Open with Vertical Jump — two linked rises, final stick as the controlled lower-body force anchor. Link a controlled contact or plant to the next clear force action.

**Quality marker:** Main observation: Vertical Jump — two linked rises, final stick. Within its listed attempts, record the declared starting position, side, movement/receiving control and a stable finish. Also record the partner task in the first connection under its own criteria; do not treat unlike drills as equivalent force tests. A miss consumes the attempt; no additional testing slots or retries are added.

**Connections:**

- **E1 + E2 — Contact and plant into projection:** Vertical Jump — two linked rises, final stick and Medicine Ball Receive-Step Chest Pass — one controlled plant: Use the controlled foot contact or received-ball plant to organize the next projection, then regain balance before the next sequence.
- **E3 + E4 — Clear onset from organized support:** Kettlebell Deadlift — rapid full-foot rise, no swing and Band Row — two-arm pull from a spoken cue: Set the support and load before the cued draw or grounded leg rise, finishing without an added jump or torso turn.
- **E5 + E6 — Hip initiation into hand force:** Low roundhouse through an empty direction gate and Handoff-to-step rotational medicine-ball scoop return: Coach handoff followed by one short upper step and a low kick through an empty direction gate target controlled placement.

### 1. Access & Prepare 1

Complete the existing predetermined Access & Prepare 1, unchanged and supplied separately. Its exercises, duration and workload are not recreated or assumed. Review its actual demands before this full-body class.

### 2. Explosiveness — Exactly 6 Exercises

Six separate explosive tasks: two upper force, two lower force, one upper rotation and one lower rotation. The opener is today’s main quality observation; every other task has a specific supporting job. Use bodyweight/light resistance and rapid intent with controlled execution, preserving the written reset and side/set recovery. Extend rest to 2–3 minutes when needed. A miss consumes the attempt; no bonus test or make-up reps.

Stop for pain, unsafe implement path, trapped support knee, uncontrolled reception, lost alignment or clear slowing. Regress only remaining work when control returns; otherwise log the stopped slot. Source alternative doses are capped by the remaining combined dose. No new flight is added by the rotational selections.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Specific job in this workout |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Vertical Jump — two linked rises, final stick** | **2 × 2** | Main controlled knee/hip force connection between two upward projections. Each round contains two modest vertical jumps. Use one dip for the first, receive on both feet and make one controlled second rise; then hold the final landing 2 s. Do not rush for short contact time or add a bounce. Use instead: two separated countermovement jumps per round with a 15 s reset when first reception cannot support a controlled second takeoff. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between complete set rounds; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 11 · E1](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_11.md). |
| E2 | **Medicine Ball Receive-Step Chest Pass — one controlled plant** | **1 × 2 per lead foot** | Primary integration of received load, a single plant and straight-ahead release. Coach softly feeds to a stationary athlete; receive at chest, make one small forward step and release as the foot plants. Stop balanced. One receiving/step/release cycle per rep, then fully reset. Use instead: the stationary catch-set chest pass if receiving or plant control is unconfirmed; if receiving itself is unsuitable use a self-started step pass or stationary pass and log zero catches as appropriate. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between lead foot bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 11 · E1](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_11.md). |
| E3 | **Kettlebell Deadlift — rapid full-foot rise, no swing** | **1 × 3** | Drive a light external load upward through hip/knee extension while retaining whole-foot pressure. Place a light bell between the feet on the floor only if its handle height permits a controlled hinge without rounding. Stand quickly with arms straight and heels down; lower for 3 s to a quiet park and fully reset. No jump, shrug or swing. Use instead: three rapid bodyweight hip-hinge returns with heels down and the same reset/rest if handle height or minimum load is unsuitable. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 10 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower Body Force Generation · source Class 11 · E4](../lower_body_force_generation/classes/class_11.md). |
| E4 | **Band Row — two-arm pull from a spoken cue** | **1 × 3** | Organize a quick backward draw from a ready still position. Permitted seated foot anchoring; hold long arms with light tension and pull on a simple spoken cue after 2–4 s. Torso stays still, return 2 s. Use instead: a self-initiated paused band row if waiting for the cue causes tension loss or rocking. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 5 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper Body Force Generation · source Class 11 · E3](../upper_body_force_generation/classes/class_11.md). |
| E5 | **Low roundhouse through an empty direction gate** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Directly preserve hip-led acceleration while controlling the kick direction and recovery endpoint. The coach identifies an empty low kick line with cones placed outside the sweep and a visual height reference from outside reach. From a stationary rear-leg stance, kick through that line below hip height, keep the knee soft, recoil and recover. Nothing is struck. A qualified coach must establish the low noncontact execution. Keep the foot below hip height, support foot turning with the pelvis, knee softly extended and recoil active; no target impact, jump, spin or locked-knee snap. Use instead: a wider visual window or shorter arc instead if accuracy causes knee lockout or leaning; target accuracy alone is not a valid rep. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 60 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 20 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Lower-body rotational power · source Class 11 · E2](../rotational_explosiveness_lower_body/classes/class_11.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |
| E6 | **Handoff-to-step rotational medicine-ball scoop return** | **1 × 2 per side/direction** | Establish grip, plant a deliberate step, then project through rotation. Receive the coach’s handoff near the rear hip while stationary. After the coach fully releases and steps clear, take one small lead-foot step, plant, pivot and scoop toward the low target; hold the finish. Use a light grippable ball and a verified low target in a permitted, fully contained release area; keep the entire flight, rebound and roll clear of people. Stay grounded, and do not receive a rebound. Keep all entry and finish footwork inside a cleared 1-metre start bay; no hop, jump or sprint. Use instead: the self-held step-in scoop instead if handoff timing or feeder clearance is unreliable. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side/direction bouts; 4 total reps; 15 s between individual reps; 90 s before the next exercise/section. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 11 · E1](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_11.md). **Combined dose:** Rotate source drills into one small support slot beside four sagittal force entries; Stage 6 consolidation is reduced again; never increase a source dose already below the combined cap. |

| P6 | **Cable / Band Lift — standing band execution** | **1 × 5 per side/direction** | Keep the working load at 2–3 technical reps/cycles in reserve. Load the low-to-high turning path from the hip to the hands and control its return, supporting the selected rising or scooping upper rotational action. Secure a band at a verified low anchor behind one side. Stand in a comfortable split stance. Guide both hands from the rear hip across toward the opposite upper chest, allowing a small foot-and-hip turn. Drive in 1–2 seconds and return in 3 seconds. Keep ribs stacked and stop below any range that requires leaning back or shrugging. No cable machine is assumed. Use instead: Reduce resistance or range if the athlete leans back or loses a controlled return. If a suitable secure low anchor is unavailable, stop and re-audit the slot; do not improvise an unsafe anchor. Apply this change only to the same remaining combined dose and recovery; it replaces the original execution and adds no extra task or make-up attempts. This replacement uses only the remaining combined dose/rest unless explicitly stated; log changed support, event type, load and added time. **Recovery:** 90 s between side bouts; 10 total reps. **Source:** [Upper-body rotational power · source Class 11 · P5](../rotational_explosiveness_upper_body/classes/class_11.md). **Combined dose:** One smaller challenging support set beside four retained force-primary entries; no automatic load increase. Stage 6 uses four reps per side or four alternating traverses. |

**Preparation sets, separate from working:**

- **P1 — Goblet Squat — dumbbell:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 5 then 1 × 3; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 90 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P2 — Floor Press — two dumbbells, neutral grip:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 6 then 1 × 3. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 90 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P3 — Standing Dumbbell Calf Raise — straight knees:** Load preparation of this same lift: 1 × 4; very light first, intermediate only if a second set is listed; at least 5 technical reps/cycles in reserve; 60 s between preparation rounds; 60 s before working sets. Extra load-finding work adds actual workload/time.
- **P4 — Dumbbell Rear-Delt Row — bilateral, hinged:** Load preparation, separate from working sets: 1 × 5. Start very light; a second set is intermediate. Keep at least 5 technical reps in reserve. Rest 60 seconds between preparation rounds, then 60 seconds before working sets. Extra load-finding reps add actual workload and time.
- **P5 — Seated band hip internal rotation:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.
- **P6 — Cable / Band Lift — standing band execution:** Preparation of this same lift: 1 × 3 per side, very light at ≥5 technical RIR; 30 s between sides; 60 s before working. Use the same controlled movement/return tempo; extra ramps add actual work and time.

**Workload:** 4 nonrotational and 4 rotational ball releases, including 0 nonrotational downward slams and 0 rotational slams already included in those release totals; 4 source ball-receiving events and 4 rotational handoffs counted separately. 4 bilateral / 0 unilateral foot-landings (8 individual landing-foot contacts), with 0 low-amplitude events already included; 0 unilateral takeoffs. 0 bilateral hand-receptions / 0 hand contacts. 3 grounded rapid leg reps, 4 grounded lower-rotation efforts (including 4 kick actions) and 0 retained/unloaded upper-rotation efforts. 0 short starts / 0 accelerating metres; 0 counted lower source-force walking-entry steps and 4 counted upper source-force task steps. Rotational entry/recovery foot placements belong to complete rotational attempts and are not separately enumerated. Primary: 7 rounds / 9 side-specific bouts, 51 conventional/one-way-traverse reps plus 0 complete walkout cycles (0 heel placements). Preparation: 38 reps/traverses plus 0 walkout cycles, separate from work. Light supports: 16 reps/cycles. These mixed units are prescribed descriptions, not equivalent stress or actual completed work.
